| `11-12-7` | MAY | A user agent may (but need not) include Accept-family request header fields describing its preferences to improve the server's selection. <br>_(user-agent,client [CLIENT])_ | the user agent MAY include request header fields (Accept, Accept-Language, Accept-Encoding, etc.) which describe its preferences for such a response. | Client API must allow callers to set Accept/Accept-Language/Accept-Encoding etc., but must never require or auto-inject them as mandatory. |
| `11-12-8` | informative | The request-header fields defined for server-driven negotiation are Accept, Accept-Charset, Accept-Encoding, Accept-Language, and User-Agent. <br>_(user-agent,client,server [CLIENT])_ | request-header fields for enabling server-driven negotiation through description of user agent capabilities and user preferences: Accept (section 14.1), Accept-Charset (section 14.2), Accept-Encoding (section 14.3), Accept-Language (section 14.4), and User-Agent (section 14.43). | Emit these five as ordinary request headers with no special-case handling; their detailed syntax is governed by sections 14.1-14.4 and 14.43. |
| `11-12-9` | MAY | An origin server may vary its response on any aspect of the request, not only the enumerated Accept-family dimensions. <br>_(origin,server [not-client])_ | an origin server is not limited to these dimensions and MAY vary the response based on any aspect of the request | No client obligation; only a cache layer would need to honor arbitrary variance (via Vary). |
| `11-12-10` | informative | The Vary header field expresses which request parameters the server used to select a server-driven-negotiated representation. <br>_(server,cache [not-client])_ | The Vary header field can be used to express the parameters the server uses to select a representation that is subject to server-driven negotiation. | A non-caching client may ignore Vary; if a response cache is ever added, Vary must key cache entries (see sections 13.6 and 14.44). |
| `11-12-11` | informative | Agent-driven negotiation is defined as the user agent selecting the best representation, after an initial response listing available representations each identified by its own URI. <br>_(user-agent,client [CLIENT])_ | With agent-driven negotiation, selection of the best representation for a response is performed by the user agent after receiving an initial response from the origin server. | No mandatory behavior; the library should surface the initial response (e.g. 300 body, alternate URIs) to the caller so the caller can re-request a chosen variant. |
| `11-12-12` | informative | In agent-driven negotiation, selection among representations may be automatic (if the user agent is capable) or manual by the user. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | Selection from among the representations may be performed automatically (if the user agent is capable of doing so) or manually by the user selecting from a generated (possibly hypertext) menu. | Nothing mandatory: automatic variant selection is permitted but not required; leaving the choice to the caller is conforming. |
| `11-12-13` | informative | RFC 2616 defines no mechanism for automatic variant selection in agent-driven negotiation, but permits such mechanisms as extensions. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | this specification does not define any mechanism for supporting automatic selection, though it also does not prevent any such mechanism from being developed as an extension and used within HTTP/1.1. | No conformance obligation; absence of automatic selection logic is fully conforming. |
| `11-12-14` | informative | The 300 (Multiple Choices) and 406 (Not Acceptable) status codes exist to enable agent-driven negotiation when the server cannot or will not do server-driven negotiation. <br>_(server,user-agent,client [CLIENT])_ | HTTP/1.1 defines the 300 (Multiple Choices) and 406 (Not Acceptable) status codes for enabling agent-driven negotiation when the server is unwilling or unable to provide a varying response using server-driven negotiation. | Client must accept 300 and 406 as valid statuses, parse their entity bodies normally, and surface them to the caller without treating them as protocol errors. |
| `11-12-15` | informative | Transparent negotiation is defined as a combination of server-driven and agent-driven negotiation performed by a cache. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | Transparent negotiation is a combination of both server-driven and agent-driven negotiation. | Definition only; irrelevant to a cacheless client. |
| `11-12-16` | informative | A cache that fully understands the dimensions of variance and holds the representation list may perform server-driven negotiation on behalf of the origin for subsequent requests. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | then the cache becomes capable of performing server-driven negotiation on behalf of the origin server for subsequent requests on that resource. | Cache-only capability; no obligation for a non-caching client. |
| `11-12-17` | informative | RFC 2616 defines no transparent negotiation mechanism, but permits one as an extension within HTTP/1.1. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | This specification does not define any mechanism for transparent negotiation, though it also does not prevent any such mechanism from being developed as an extension that could be used within HTTP/1.1. | No conformance obligation; nothing to implement or test. |

### 13.1-13.3 - RFC 2616 Sections 13.1-13.3 — Caching: Correctness, Warnings, Expiration Model, Validation Model

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `13.1-13.3-1` | informative | Semantic transparency may be relaxed by a client or origin server only through an explicit protocol-level request. <br>_(client,origin [CLIENT])_ | the protocol requires that transparency be relaxed - only by an explicit protocol-level request when relaxed by client or origin server | Any transparency-relaxing client behavior (e.g., accepting stale) must be expressed via explicit Cache-Control request directives, never done silently. |
| `13.1-13.3-2` | informative | When a cache or client relaxes transparency, it must be accompanied by an explicit warning to the end user. <br>_(client,cache [CLIENT])_ | only with an explicit warning to the end user when relaxed by cache or client | Surface staleness/Warning information to the API caller whenever the client knowingly returns non-transparent results. |
| `13.1-13.3-3` | informative | It must be possible for clients to detect any potential relaxation of semantic transparency. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | it must be possible for the clients to detect any potential relaxation of semantic transparency. | Expose received Warning and Age headers unmodified to callers so relaxation is always detectable. |
| `13.1-13.3-4` | MUST | A correct cache must respond with the most up-to-date appropriate stored response that is revalidated, fresh enough, or an appropriate 304/305/4xx/5xx message. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | A correct cache MUST respond to a request with the most up-to-date response held by the cache that is appropriate to the request | No client action (poseidon has no cache layer); if a private cache is added, serve only revalidated, fresh-enough, or appropriate 304/305/error entries. |
| `13.1-13.3-5` | informative | In the default case, 'fresh enough' means meeting the least restrictive freshness requirement of client, origin server, and cache; origin server can make its requirement alone govern. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | it meets the least restrictive freshness requirement of the client, origin server, and cache | No client action; a client-side cache must combine client/origin/cache freshness constraints per this rule. |
| `13.1-13.3-6` | MAY | A cache may return a response that is not fresh enough (with a Warning header) unless prohibited by directives such as no-store or no-cache. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the cache MAY still return the response with the appropriate Warning header (see section 13.1.5 and 14.46), unless such a response is prohibited | No client action; if caching added, stale serving must attach Warning and honor no-store/no-cache prohibitions. |
| `13.1-13.3-7` | SHOULD | If the cache cannot reach the origin server, it should respond from cache when the response can be correctly served. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | a correct cache SHOULD respond as above if the response can be correctly served from the cache | No client action; cache-only disconnected-operation rule. |
| `13.1-13.3-8` | MUST | If the cache cannot serve the response correctly during a communication failure, it must return an error or warning indicating the failure. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | if not it MUST return an error or warning indicating that there was a communication failure. | No client action; cache must signal communication failure (e.g., 504/Warning 112) rather than serve incorrect content. |
| `13.1-13.3-9` | SHOULD | A cache forwarding a received response that went stale in transit should forward it without adding a new Warning and without removing existing Warnings. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the cache SHOULD forward it to the requesting client without adding a new Warning (but without removing any existing Warning headers). | No client action; cache forwarding rule — preserve existing Warning headers. |
| `13.1-13.3-10` | SHOULD NOT | A cache should not attempt revalidation merely because a response became stale in transit, to avoid infinite loops. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | A cache SHOULD NOT attempt to revalidate a response simply because that response became stale in transit | No client action; cache revalidation-loop avoidance rule. |
| `13.1-13.3-11` | MAY | A user agent receiving a stale response without a Warning may display a warning indication to the user. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | A user agent that receives a stale response without a Warning MAY display a warning indication to the user. | Optional: client may flag responses it can compute as stale (via Age/Expires/Date) even when no Warning header is present. |
| `13.1-13.3-12` | MUST | Whenever a cache returns a response that is neither first-hand nor fresh enough, it must attach a Warning general-header saying so. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | it MUST attach a warning to that effect, using a Warning general-header. | No client emission duty; client should parse and expose Warning headers so callers see cache-attached warnings. |
| `13.1-13.3-13` | MAY | Warnings may be used for purposes other than caching; a warning distinguishes such responses from true failures. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Warnings MAY be used for other purposes, both cache-related and otherwise. | Client must not treat presence of a Warning header as an error; pass it through as metadata. |
| `13.1-13.3-14` | MUST | 1xx warn-codes describe freshness/revalidation status and must be deleted from a stored cache entry after successful revalidation. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | Warnings that describe the freshness or revalidation status of the response, and so MUST be deleted after a successful revalidation. | No client action; if a cache is added, strip 1xx warn-codes from entries after successful revalidation. |
| `13.1-13.3-15` | MAY | 1xx warn-codes may be generated by a cache only when validating a cached entry. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | 1XX warn-codes MAY be generated by a cache only when validating a cached entry. | No client action; caches may only emit 1xx warnings in the validation path. |
| `13.1-13.3-16` | MUST NOT | Clients must never generate 1xx warn-codes. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | It MUST NOT be generated by clients. | Verify the client never emits Warning headers with 1xx warn-codes in requests or in any synthesized response it hands to callers. |
| `13.1-13.3-17` | MUST NOT | 2xx warn-codes describe entity aspects not rectified by revalidation and must not be deleted after successful revalidation. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | which MUST NOT be deleted after a successful revalidation. | No client action; if a cache is added, 2xx warn-codes persist across revalidation. |
| `13.1-13.3-18` | informative | Warnings passed through HTTP/1.0 caches carry a warning-date field that prevents an HTTP/1.1 recipient from believing an erroneously cached Warning. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Warnings in responses that are passed to HTTP/1.0 caches carry an extra warning-date field, which prevents a future HTTP/1.1 recipient from believing an erroneously cached Warning. | Warning parser must accept the optional warn-date field; a recipient should disregard warnings whose warn-date differs from the response Date (per 14.46). |
| `13.1-13.3-19` | MAY | Warning text may be in any appropriate natural language and may include an optional character-set indication. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The text MAY be in any appropriate natural language (perhaps based on the client's Accept headers), and include an OPTIONAL indication of what character set is used. | Warning parser must tolerate arbitrary warn-text bytes and an optional charset indication without failing. |
| `13.1-13.3-20` | MAY | Multiple warnings, including duplicates of the same code, may be attached to one response by origin server or cache. <br>_(origin,cache [CLIENT])_ | Multiple warnings MAY be attached to a response (either by the origin server or by a cache), including multiple warnings with the same code number. | Response parsing must handle repeated Warning headers and duplicate warn-codes without collapsing or erroring. |
| `13.1-13.3-21` | informative | On any apparent conflict between caching header values, the most restrictive interpretation is applied. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | if there is any apparent conflict between header values, the most restrictive interpretation is applied | Any client-side interpretation of Cache-Control/Expires conflicts must pick the most restrictive (transparency-preserving) reading. |
| `13.1-13.3-22` | SHOULD NOT | A user agent should not default to non-transparent behavior or behavior causing abnormally ineffective caching. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | The user agent SHOULD NOT default to either non-transparent behavior, or behavior that results in abnormally ineffective caching | Default client configuration must not inject directives like max-stale or forced-revalidation on every request. |
| `13.1-13.3-23` | MAY | A user agent may be explicitly configured by the user to override basic caching mechanisms. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | but MAY be explicitly configured to do so by an explicit action of the user. | Overrides (e.g., always adding max-stale) are acceptable only as explicit opt-in configuration by the caller. |
| `13.1-13.3-24` | SHOULD | If the user overrode caching, the user agent should indicate whenever displayed information might not meet transparency requirements (e.g., known-stale entity). <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | the user agent SHOULD explicitly indicate to the user whenever this results in the display of information that might not meet the server's transparency requirements | Library equivalent: expose per-response staleness metadata so the embedding application can indicate it to end users. |
| `13.1-13.3-25` | SHOULD | If the user's override abnormally reduces cache effectiveness, the user agent should continually indicate this state to the user. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | the user agent SHOULD continually indicate this state to the user | Library equivalent: make cache-defeating configuration visibly discoverable (documented flag/state), not silent. |
| `13.1-13.3-26` | MAY | A cache operator may configure the cache to return stale responses even when not requested by clients. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the operator of a cache MAY choose to configure it to return stale responses even when not requested by clients. | No client action; cache-operator provision. |
| `13.1-13.3-27` | MUST | Whenever a cache returns a stale response it must mark it as stale using a Warning header. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | Whenever a cache returns a stale response, it MUST mark it as such (using a Warning header) | No client emission duty; client can rely on Warning (110) to detect stale responses — ensure Warning is parsed and exposed. |
| `13.1-13.3-28` | SHOULD NOT | A cache should not return a stale response when the client explicitly requests a first-hand or fresh one, unless compliance is impossible. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | a cache SHOULD NOT return a stale response if the client explicitly requests a first-hand or fresh one, unless it is impossible to comply for technical or policy reasons. | No client action; supports client use of request directives (max-age=0, no-cache) to demand freshness. |
| `13.1-13.3-29` | MAY | A client request may specify the maximum age it will accept for an unvalidated response; zero forces revalidation by all caches. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client's request MAY specify the maximum age it is willing to accept of an unvalidated response; specifying a value of zero forces the cache(s) to revalidate all responses. | Support emitting Cache-Control: max-age=N (including max-age=0) as a request header without mangling it. |
| `13.1-13.3-30` | MAY | A client may specify the minimum freshness time remaining before a response expires (min-fresh). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MAY also specify the minimum time remaining before a response expires. | Support emitting Cache-Control: min-fresh=N request directive. |
| `13.1-13.3-31` | MAY | A client may specify that it will accept stale responses up to some maximum staleness (max-stale). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MAY also specify that it will accept stale responses, up to some maximum amount of staleness. | Support emitting Cache-Control: max-stale[=N] request directive when the caller opts in. |
| `13.1-13.3-32` | MAY | An explicit future expiration time from the origin server indicates the response may be used by caches to satisfy subsequent requests without contacting the server. <br>_(origin,cache [not-client])_ | provide an explicit expiration time in the future, indicating that a response MAY be used to satisfy subsequent requests. | No client action; the basic cache-reuse permission granted by Expires/max-age. |
| `13.1-13.3-33` | informative | The expiration mechanism applies only to responses taken from a cache, not to first-hand responses forwarded immediately to the requesting client. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The expiration mechanism applies only to responses taken from a cache and not to first-hand responses forwarded immediately to the requesting client. | Client must deliver first-hand responses to the caller even if they arrive already expired; never reject a response for being stale on receipt. |
| `13.1-13.3-34` | MAY | An origin server may assign an explicit expiration time in the past to make a response always stale. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | If an origin server wishes to force a semantically transparent cache to validate every request, it MAY assign an explicit expiration time in the past. | No client action; client must tolerate past/invalid Expires values (treated as already expired). |
| `13.1-13.3-35` | SHOULD | A cache holding an always-stale response should validate it before using it for subsequent requests. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the cache SHOULD validate it before using it for subsequent requests. | No client action; cache validation duty for pre-expired entries. |
| `13.1-13.3-36` | SHOULD | An origin server wanting to force every HTTP/1.1 cache to validate each request should use the must-revalidate cache-control directive. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | it SHOULD use the "must-revalidate" cache-control directive (see section 14.9). | No client action; server-side directive choice. |
| `13.1-13.3-37` | informative | Servers convey explicit expiration via either the Expires header or the Cache-Control max-age directive. <br>_(server,origin [CLIENT])_ | Servers specify explicit expiration times using either the Expires header, or the max-age directive of the Cache-Control header. | Any client-side freshness evaluation must recognize both Expires and Cache-Control: max-age as expiration sources. |
| `13.1-13.3-38` | informative | An expiration time cannot force a user agent to refresh its display or reload; its semantics apply only to caching mechanisms. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | An expiration time cannot be used to force a user agent to refresh its display or reload a resource; its semantics apply only to caching mechanisms | Do not couple expiration status with any reload/history behavior; check expiration only when a new request is initiated. |
| `13.1-13.3-39` | informative | HTTP/1.1 does not prescribe heuristic expiration algorithms but imposes worst-case constraints on their results. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | The HTTP/1.1 specification does not provide specific algorithms, but does impose worst-case constraints on their results. | No client action; any added heuristic freshness logic must respect the worst-case constraints in 13.2.4. |
| `13.1-13.3-40` | SHOULD | Hosts using HTTP, especially origin servers and caches, should synchronize clocks via NTP or similar. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | SHOULD use NTP [28] or some similar protocol to synchronize their clocks to a globally accurate time standard. | Document that age/freshness math assumes a reasonably synchronized host clock; use one consistent local clock for all calculations. |
| `13.1-13.3-41` | informative | HTTP/1.1 requires origin servers to send a Date header with every response when possible. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | HTTP/1.1 requires origin servers to send a Date header, if possible, with every response | No client emission duty; client parsing must tolerate responses that lack a Date header. |
| `13.1-13.3-42` | informative | The Age response-header conveys the sender's estimate of time since the response was generated or revalidated at the origin. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | HTTP/1.1 uses the Age response-header to convey the estimated age of the response message when obtained from a cache. | Parse the Age response header as non-negative delta-seconds and expose it to callers. |
| `13.1-13.3-43` | informative | Age computed as now minus date_value must be clamped: a negative result is replaced by zero. <br>_(cache,client [CLIENT])_ | now minus date_value, if the local clock is reasonably well synchronized to the origin server's clock. If the result is negative, the result is replaced by zero. | If the client computes apparent age, clamp (now - date_value) at zero to absorb clock skew. |
| `13.1-13.3-44` | informative | The two independent age estimates are combined as corrected_received_age = max(now - date_value, age_value). <br>_(cache,client [CLIENT])_ | corrected_received_age = max(now - date_value, age_value) | Age computation must take the maximum of clock-based and Age-header-based estimates, never one alone. |
| `13.1-13.3-45` | MUST | A received Age value must be interpreted relative to the time the request was initiated, not the time the response was received. <br>_(cache,client [CLIENT])_ | when an Age value is received, it MUST be interpreted relative to the time the request was initiated, not the time that the response was received. | Record request_time at send; add (response_time - request_time) network delay into any age calculation on received Age values. |
| `13.1-13.3-46` | informative | Summary age algorithm on receipt: apparent_age clamped at zero, combined with age_value, then corrected by measured response delay. <br>_(cache,client [CLIENT])_ | apparent_age = max(0, response_time - date_value); corrected_received_age = max(apparent_age, age_value); response_delay = response_time - request_time; corrected_initial_age = corrected_received_age + response_delay; | Implement this exact formula sequence if the client evaluates response age (needed for any freshness/staleness reporting). |
| `13.1-13.3-47` | informative | Current age adds resident time since receipt: current_age = corrected_initial_age + resident_time, where resident_time = now - response_time. <br>_(cache,client [CLIENT])_ | resident_time = now - response_time; current_age = corrected_initial_age + resident_time; | When holding a response, ongoing age must include time since receipt, not just the value computed at arrival. |
| `13.1-13.3-48` | MUST | When generating a response from a cache entry, the cache must include exactly one Age header equal to the entry's current_age. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the cache MUST include a single Age header field in the response with a value equal to the cache entry's current_age. | No client action; if a private cache serving stored responses is added, it must attach a single computed Age header. |
| `13.1-13.3-49` | informative | Presence of an Age header implies the response is not first-hand; absence does not imply first-hand unless the whole path is HTTP/1.1. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The presence of an Age header field in a response implies that a response is not first-hand. However, the converse is not true | Client may flag responses with Age as cached/non-first-hand; must never assume first-hand solely from Age absence. |
| `13.1-13.3-50` | informative | The max-age directive takes priority over Expires: if max-age is present, freshness_lifetime = max_age_value. <br>_(cache,client [CLIENT])_ | The max-age directive takes priority over Expires, so if max-age is present in a response, the calculation is simply: freshness_lifetime = max_age_value | Freshness evaluation must prefer Cache-Control: max-age over Expires whenever both appear. |
| `13.1-13.3-51` | informative | Without max-age, if Expires is present, freshness_lifetime = expires_value - date_value. <br>_(cache,client [CLIENT])_ | Otherwise, if Expires is present in the response, the calculation is: freshness_lifetime = expires_value - date_value | Compute Expires-based lifetime as the difference against the response Date, not against the local clock. |
| `13.1-13.3-52` | MAY | If none of Expires, max-age, or s-maxage is present and no other caching restrictions apply, a cache may compute a heuristic freshness lifetime. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the cache MAY compute a freshness lifetime using a heuristic. | No client action; heuristic freshness is a cache option, permitted only absent explicit expiry information. |
| `13.1-13.3-53` | MUST | A cache using heuristic freshness must attach Warning 113 to any response whose age exceeds 24 hours, if not already present. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | The cache MUST attach Warning 113 to any response whose age is more than 24 hours if such warning has not already been added. | No client action; if a heuristic-caching layer is added, enforce the 24-hour Warning 113 rule. |
| `13.1-13.3-54` | SHOULD | If the response has a Last-Modified time, the heuristic expiration value should be no more than some fraction (typically 10%) of the interval since that time. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the heuristic expiration value SHOULD be no more than some fraction of the interval since that time. | No client action; heuristic lifetime cap for caches (typical fraction 10%). |
| `13.1-13.3-55` | informative | A response is fresh exactly when its freshness lifetime exceeds its current age: response_is_fresh = (freshness_lifetime > current_age). <br>_(cache,client [CLIENT])_ | response_is_fresh = (freshness_lifetime > current_age) | Use strict greater-than comparison of lifetime vs current age for any client-side freshness determination. |
| `13.1-13.3-56` | MAY | A client receiving a non-first-hand response whose Date is older than the Date of its own already-fresh cache entry may ignore the response. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | the Date header in its existing cache entry is newer than the Date on the new response, then the client MAY ignore the response. | If a client cache exists, compare Date headers and permit discarding an older non-first-hand response in favor of the newer cached one. |
| `13.1-13.3-57` | MAY | A client ignoring such a response may retry with a Cache-Control: max-age=0 directive to force an origin check. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If so, it MAY retry the request with a "Cache-Control: max-age=0" directive (see section 14.9), to force a check with the origin server. | Support retrying a request with Cache-Control: max-age=0 added to bypass intermediate cache freshness. |
| `13.1-13.3-58` | MUST | A cache holding two fresh responses for the same representation with different validators must use the one with the more recent Date header. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | If a cache has two fresh responses for the same representation with different validators, it MUST use the one with the more recent Date header. | No client action; if a private cache is added, entry selection must prefer the more recent Date on validator conflict. |
| `13.1-13.3-59` | informative | Date values order responses only to a granularity of one second. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The Date values are ordered to a granularity of one second. | Response-ordering logic based on Date must not assume sub-second resolution; equal-second Dates are unordered. |
| `13.1-13.3-60` | SHOULD | If a revalidation response carries a Date older than the existing entry's Date, the client should repeat the request unconditionally with max-age=0 or no-cache. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | the client SHOULD repeat the request unconditionally, and include Cache-Control: max-age=0 | On revalidation returning an older-Date response, re-issue the request unconditionally with Cache-Control: max-age=0 (or no-cache) to reach the origin. |
| `13.1-13.3-61` | MAY | If the Date values of two responses are equal, the client may use either response or prudently request a new one. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If the Date values are equal, then the client MAY use either response (or MAY, if it is being extremely prudent, request a new response). | Equal-Date disambiguation may pick either response; no deterministic choice is required. |
| `13.1-13.3-62` | MUST NOT | Servers must not depend on clients deterministically choosing between responses generated during the same second with overlapping expiration. <br>_(server,origin [not-client])_ | Servers MUST NOT depend on clients being able to choose deterministically between responses generated during the same second, if their expiration times overlap. | No client action; guarantees client's non-deterministic equal-Date choice (fact 61) is acceptable. |
| `13.1-13.3-63` | informative | A client making a conditional request for a resource it has cached includes the associated validator in the request. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | When a client (user agent or proxy cache) makes a conditional request for a resource for which it has a cache entry, it includes the associated validator in the request. | Conditional-request support must carry the stored validator (ETag or Last-Modified) in the appropriate If-* header. |
| `13.1-13.3-64` | informative | When the presented validator matches, the server responds with a special status (usually 304 Not Modified) and no entity-body. <br>_(server,origin [CLIENT])_ | if they match (see section 13.3.3), it responds with a special status code (usually, 304 (Not Modified)) and no entity-body. | Response framing must treat 304 as bodiless: do not read an entity-body after a 304 status line/headers. |
| `13.1-13.3-65` | informative | A conditional request is identical to a normal request except it carries a special header (containing the validator) that makes the method conditional. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | a conditional request looks exactly the same as a normal request for the same resource, except that it carries a special header (which includes the validator) | Emit conditional requests as ordinary requests plus the conditional header; no other wire differences. |
| `13.1-13.3-66` | informative | A response lacking a validator may still be cached and served until expiry unless a cache-control directive prohibits it, but it cannot be revalidated afterward. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | a response that lacks a validator may still be cached, and served from cache until it expires, unless this is explicitly prohibited by a cache-control directive. | No client action; validator absence limits refreshability, not initial cacheability. |
| `13.1-13.3-67` | informative | With Last-Modified as validator, a cache entry is considered valid if the entity has not been modified since the Last-Modified value. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | a cache entry is considered to be valid if the entity has not been modified since the Last-Modified value. | Store Last-Modified from responses and use it as the modification-time validator in If-Modified-Since. |
| `13.1-13.3-68` | informative | The ETag response-header value is an entity tag providing an opaque cache validator. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The ETag response-header field value, an entity tag, provides for an "opaque" cache validator. | Treat ETag values as opaque byte strings: store and echo exactly, never parse or normalize their contents. |
| `13.1-13.3-69` | OPTIONAL | Support for weak validators is optional. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Support for weak validators is optional. | Weak-validator support may be omitted, but the W/ prefix must still be recognized so weak tags are not misused where prohibited (fact 73). |
| `13.1-13.3-70` | informative | Strong validators are usable in any context; weak validators only in contexts not depending on exact entity equality. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Strong validators are usable in any context. Weak validators are only usable in contexts that do not depend on exact equality of an entity. | Classify each conditional-request context by whether exact equality is required before allowing a weak validator. |
| `13.1-13.3-71` | informative | Only a strong validator is usable for a sub-range retrieval, otherwise the client might assemble an internally inconsistent entity. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | only a strong validator is usable for a sub-range retrieval, since otherwise the client might end up with an internally inconsistent entity. | Never combine Range requests with a weak validator (W/ ETag or non-strong Last-Modified) for resuming/completing an entity. |
| `13.1-13.3-72` | MAY | Clients may issue simple (non-subrange) GET requests with either weak or strong validators. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Clients MAY issue simple (non-subrange) GET requests with either weak validators or strong validators. | Allow both W/ and strong ETags in If-None-Match on plain GET requests. |
| `13.1-13.3-73` | MUST NOT | Clients must not use weak validators in any request form other than simple non-subrange GET. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Clients MUST NOT use weak validators in other forms of request. | Reject or refuse to send weak validators in If-Match, If-Range, range requests, or non-GET conditionals; add a conformance test. |
| `13.1-13.3-74` | MUST | Strong comparison: validators are equal only if identical in every way and neither is weak. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | in order to be considered equal, both validators MUST be identical in every way, and both MUST NOT be weak. | Implement strong comparison as byte-identical plus both-not-weak for any client-side validator matching (e.g., range-resume decisions). |
| `13.1-13.3-75` | MUST | Weak comparison: validators are equal if identical in every way, with weak tagging on either or both not affecting the result. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | in order to be considered equal, both validators MUST be identical in every way, but either or both of them MAY be tagged as "weak" without affecting the result. | Implement weak comparison as byte-identical opaque values ignoring the W/ prefix. |
| `13.1-13.3-76` | informative | An entity tag is strong unless explicitly tagged as weak. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | An entity tag is strong unless it is explicitly tagged as weak. | ETag parsing must detect the W/ weak prefix and default to strong when absent. |
| `13.1-13.3-77` | informative | A Last-Modified time used as a request validator is implicitly weak unless rules allow deducing it is strong. <br>_(client,origin,cache [CLIENT])_ | A Last-Modified time, when used as a validator in a request, is implicitly weak unless it is possible to deduce that it is strong | Treat stored Last-Modified as weak by default; apply the strongness deduction rules before any use requiring a strong validator. |
| `13.1-13.3-78` | informative | Client-side strongness rule: a Last-Modified used in If-Modified-Since/If-Unmodified-Since is strong only if the cache entry has a Date value and the Last-Modified is at least 60 seconds before that Date. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The presented Last-Modified time is at least 60 seconds before the Date value. | Deduce Last-Modified strength as: cached entry has Date, and (Date - Last-Modified) >= 60s. |
| `13.1-13.3-79` | MAY | An implementation may use a guard value larger than 60 seconds for the Last-Modified strongness rule. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | An implementation MAY use a value larger than 60 seconds, if it is believed that 60 seconds is too short. | The 60-second threshold may be raised (config or constant), never lowered. |
| `13.1-13.3-80` | MAY | A client with only a Last-Modified time (no opaque validator) may perform a sub-range retrieval only if that Last-Modified is strong per the deduction rules. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | it MAY do this only if the Last-Modified time is strong in the sense described here. | Gate Range+conditional retrieval on the Last-Modified strongness check when no ETag is available. |
| `13.1-13.3-81` | MUST | A cache or origin server receiving any conditional request other than a full-body GET must use the strong comparison function. <br>_(cache,origin [not-client])_ | A cache or origin server receiving a conditional request, other than a full-body GET request, MUST use the strong comparison function to evaluate the condition. | No client action; explains why weak validators in non-GET/range conditionals (fact 73) cannot match. |
| `13.1-13.3-82` | SHOULD | HTTP/1.1 origin servers should send an entity tag validator unless it is not feasible to generate one. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | SHOULD send an entity tag validator unless it is not feasible to generate one. | No client action; client must handle responses with and without ETag. |
| `13.1-13.3-83` | MAY | Origin servers may send a weak entity tag instead of a strong one for performance or feasibility reasons. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | MAY send a weak entity tag instead of a strong entity tag, if performance considerations support the use of weak entity tags | No client action; client must accept W/-prefixed ETags in responses. |
| `13.1-13.3-84` | SHOULD | Origin servers should send a Last-Modified value if feasible, unless its use in If-Modified-Since risks serious transparency breakdown. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | SHOULD send a Last-Modified value if it is feasible to send one | No client action; client must handle responses with and without Last-Modified. |
| `13.1-13.3-85` | MUST | To be legal, a strong entity tag must change whenever the associated entity value changes in any way. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | In order to be legal, a strong entity tag MUST change whenever the associated entity value changes in any way. | No client action; server-side validator-generation obligation the client may rely on for strong ETags. |
| `13.1-13.3-86` | SHOULD | A weak entity tag should change whenever the associated entity changes in a semantically significant way. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | A weak entity tag SHOULD change whenever the associated entity changes in a semantically significant way. | No client action; server-side weak-validator generation rule. |
| `13.1-13.3-87` | MUST | If the origin server provided an entity tag, an HTTP/1.1 client must use that entity tag in any cache-conditional request (If-Match or If-None-Match). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If an entity tag has been provided by the origin server, MUST use that entity tag in any cache-conditional request (using If-Match or If-None-Match). | When an ETag was received, conditional requests must carry it in If-None-Match/If-Match — never fall back to Last-Modified alone; add a conformance test. |
| `13.1-13.3-88` | SHOULD | If only a Last-Modified value was provided, the client should use it in non-subrange cache-conditional requests via If-Modified-Since. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If only a Last-Modified value has been provided by the origin server, SHOULD use that value in non-subrange cache-conditional requests (using If-Modified-Since). | Without an ETag, emit If-Modified-Since with the stored Last-Modified for plain conditional GETs. |
| `13.1-13.3-89` | MAY | If only a Last-Modified value was provided by an HTTP/1.0 origin server, the client may use it in subrange cache-conditional requests via If-Unmodified-Since. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If only a Last-Modified value has been provided by an HTTP/1.0 origin server, MAY use that value in subrange cache-conditional requests (using If-Unmodified-Since:). | Optionally allow If-Unmodified-Since-guarded range requests against HTTP/1.0 origins when only Last-Modified is known. |
| `13.1-13.3-90` | SHOULD | The user agent should provide a way to disable Last-Modified-based subrange conditionals against HTTP/1.0 origins in case of difficulty. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | The user agent SHOULD provide a way to disable this, in case of difficulty. | If fact 89 behavior is implemented, expose a configuration switch to disable it. |
| `13.1-13.3-91` | SHOULD | If both an entity tag and a Last-Modified value were provided, the client should use both validators in cache-conditional requests so HTTP/1.0 and HTTP/1.1 caches respond appropriately. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If both an entity tag and a Last-Modified value have been provided by the origin server, SHOULD use both validators in cache-conditional requests. | Send both If-None-Match (ETag) and If-Modified-Since (Last-Modified) when both validators are stored. |
| `13.1-13.3-92` | MUST NOT | An HTTP/1.1 origin server receiving a conditional request with both a Last-Modified date and entity tags must not return 304 unless consistent with all conditional header fields. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | MUST NOT return a response status of 304 (Not Modified) unless doing so is consistent with all of the conditional header fields in the request. | No client action; guarantees sending both validators (fact 91) is safe — 304 implies all conditions matched. |
| `13.1-13.3-93` | MUST NOT | An HTTP/1.1 caching proxy receiving a conditional request with both a Last-Modified date and entity tags must not return a locally cached response unless it is consistent with all conditional header fields. <br>_(proxy,cache [not-client])_ | MUST NOT return a locally cached response to the client unless that cached response is consistent with all of the conditional header fields in the request. | No client action; proxy-side multi-validator consistency rule. |
| `13.1-13.3-94` | informative | Only entity tags (and Last-Modified for HTTP/1.0 compatibility) are used for cache-entry validation; comparisons of any other headers are never used for that purpose. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | comparisons of any other headers (except Last-Modified, for compatibility with HTTP/1.0) are never used for purposes of validating a cache entry. | Never construct cache-validation conditionals from headers other than ETag and Last-Modified; validator comparison is byte-equality only. |

### 13.4-13.13 - RFC 2616 Caching: Response Cacheability, Constructing Responses From Caches, Hop-by-hop Headers, Combining Headers/Ranges, Negotiated Responses, Shared Caches, Errors, Side Effects, Invalidation, Write-Through, Replacement, History Lists

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `13.4-13.13-1` | MAY | Unless constrained by a cache-control directive, a cache may always store a successful response, return it without validation while fresh, and return it after successful validation. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | a caching system MAY always store a successful response (see section 13.8) as a cache entry, MAY return it without validation if it is fresh | N/A for cache-less client; if a client-side cache is added, permit storing/serving successful responses absent prohibiting cache-control directives. |
| `13.4-13.13-2` | MAY | A response with neither a cache validator nor an explicit expiration time is not expected to be cached, but certain caches may still cache it (e.g. under poor connectivity). <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | certain caches MAY violate this expectation (for example, when little or no network connectivity is available) | No client action; be aware intermediary caches may serve validator-less responses from cache. |
| `13.4-13.13-3` | informative | A client can usually detect that a response was served from a cache by comparing the Date header to the current time. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client can usually detect that such a response was taken from a cache by comparing the Date header to the current time. | Expose the Date response header unmodified to callers so cached-vs-fresh detection by Date comparison is possible. |
| `13.4-13.13-4` | MAY | Responses with status 200, 203, 206, 300, 301 or 410 may be stored by a cache and reused, subject to expiration, unless a cache-control directive prohibits caching. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | A response received with a status code of 200, 203, 206, 300, 301 or 410 MAY be stored by a cache and used in reply to a subsequent request | N/A for cache-less client; a future client-side cache must restrict default cacheability to exactly these status codes. |
| `13.4-13.13-5` | MUST NOT | A cache that does not support the Range and Content-Range headers must not cache 206 (Partial Content) responses. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | a cache that does not support the Range and Content-Range headers MUST NOT cache 206 (Partial Content) responses. | N/A for cache-less client; if caching is added without Range/Content-Range support, refuse to store 206 responses. |
| `13.4-13.13-6` | MUST NOT | Responses with any other status code (e.g. 302, 307) must not be returned from cache unless cache-control directives or other headers (Expires, max-age, s-maxage, must-revalidate, proxy-revalidate, public, private) explicitly allow it. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | A response received with any other status code (e.g. status codes 302 and 307) MUST NOT be returned in a reply to a subsequent request | N/A for cache-less client; a client-side cache must gate non-listed status codes on explicit cacheability headers. |
| `13.4-13.13-7` | MUST | End-to-end headers in responses must be stored as part of a cache entry and must be transmitted in any response formed from that entry. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | End-to-end headers in responses MUST be stored as part of a cache entry and MUST be transmitted in any response formed from a cache entry. | N/A for cache-less client; a client-side cache must persist and replay all end-to-end headers with the cached body. |
| `13.4-13.13-8` | informative | Hop-by-hop headers are meaningful only for a single transport-level connection: they are not stored by caches and not forwarded by proxies. <br>_(cache,proxy,all [CLIENT])_ | Hop-by-hop headers, which are meaningful only for a single transport-level connection, and are not stored by caches or forwarded by proxies. | Treat hop-by-hop headers as connection-local: never carry them across connection reuse boundaries or into any stored/replayed response. |
| `13.4-13.13-9` | informative | The HTTP/1.1 hop-by-hop headers are exactly: Connection, Keep-Alive, Proxy-Authenticate, Proxy-Authorization, TE, Trailers, Transfer-Encoding, Upgrade. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The following HTTP/1.1 headers are hop-by-hop headers: | Hard-code this eight-header hop-by-hop set in header classification; verify the http1 layer never treats them as end-to-end. |
| `13.4-13.13-10` | informative | All headers defined by HTTP/1.1 other than the listed eight are end-to-end headers. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | All other headers defined by HTTP/1.1 are end-to-end headers. | Default classification for any header not in the hop-by-hop list is end-to-end; do not strip or suppress such headers. |
| `13.4-13.13-11` | MUST | Any new hop-by-hop header introduced into HTTP/1.1 or later must be listed in a Connection header. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Other hop-by-hop headers MUST be listed in a Connection header, (section 14.10) to be introduced into HTTP/1.1 (or later). | If the client emits a non-standard hop-by-hop header, name it in Connection; when receiving, treat any header named in Connection as hop-by-hop. |
| `13.4-13.13-12` | SHOULD NOT | A transparent proxy should not modify an end-to-end header unless the header's definition requires or specifically allows it. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A transparent proxy SHOULD NOT modify an end-to-end header unless the definition of that header requires or specifically allows that. | No client obligation; proxy-only rule. |
| `13.4-13.13-13` | MUST NOT | A transparent proxy must not modify, and must not add if absent, the fields Content-Location, Content-MD5, ETag, or Last-Modified in a request or response. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A transparent proxy MUST NOT modify any of the following fields in a request or response, and it MUST NOT add any of these fields if not already present | No client obligation; proxy-only rule. |
| `13.4-13.13-14` | MUST NOT | A transparent proxy must not modify the Expires field in a response. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A transparent proxy MUST NOT modify any of the following fields in a response: | No client obligation; proxy-only rule (field in question is Expires). |
| `13.4-13.13-15` | MAY | A transparent proxy may add an Expires field to a response if not already present. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | but it MAY add any of these fields if not already present. | No client obligation; proxy-only rule. |
| `13.4-13.13-16` | MUST | If a proxy adds an Expires header, its field-value must be identical to the Date header of that response. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | If an Expires header is added, it MUST be given a field-value identical to that of the Date header in that response. | No client obligation; a client may observe Expires == Date as meaning already-expired. |
| `13.4-13.13-17` | MUST NOT | A proxy must not modify or add Content-Encoding, Content-Range, or Content-Type in a message carrying the no-transform cache-control directive, nor in any request. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | MUST NOT modify or add any of the following fields in a message that contains the no-transform cache-control directive, or in any request | No client obligation; client may send Cache-Control: no-transform to invoke this protection. |
| `13.4-13.13-18` | MAY | A non-transparent proxy may modify or add Content-Encoding, Content-Range, or Content-Type in a message that does not include no-transform. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A non-transparent proxy MAY modify or add these fields to a message that does not include no-transform | No client obligation; be tolerant of transformed entity headers when traversing non-transparent proxies. |
| `13.4-13.13-19` | MUST | If a non-transparent proxy modifies or adds those fields, it must add a Warning 214 (Transformation applied) if one is not already present in the message. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | it MUST add a Warning 214 (Transformation applied) if one does not already appear in the message | No client obligation; client may surface Warning 214 to callers as a transformation indicator. |
| `13.4-13.13-20` | MAY | Future authentication mechanisms may rely on the values of end-to-end header fields not in the non-modifiable list, so unnecessary modification risks authentication failures. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Such authentication mechanisms MAY rely on the values of header fields not listed here. | No client action; cautionary note against gratuitous header modification by intermediaries. |
| `13.4-13.13-21` | informative | The Content-Length field of a request or response is added or deleted according to the message-length rules in section 4.4. <br>_(proxy,all [not-client])_ | The Content-Length field of a request or response is added or deleted according to the rules in section 4.4. | Cross-reference: client framing of Content-Length is governed by section 4.4, not by this caching section. |
| `13.4-13.13-22` | MUST | A transparent proxy must preserve the entity-length of the entity-body, although it may change the transfer-length. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A transparent proxy MUST preserve the entity-length (section 7.2.2) of the entity-body, although it MAY change the transfer-length (section 4.4). | No client obligation; client must still tolerate transfer-length changes (e.g. chunking added by a proxy) on receipt. |
| `13.4-13.13-23` | informative | When a validating request yields 304 (Not Modified), the cache uses the entity-body stored in the cache entry as the entity-body of the constructed outgoing response. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the cache uses the entity-body stored in the cache entry as the entity-body of this outgoing response | N/A for cache-less client; if the client revalidates stored responses, a 304 means reuse the stored body verbatim. |
| `13.4-13.13-24` | MAY | On a 206 response whose ETag or Last-Modified matches the cache entry exactly, the cache may combine stored contents with the newly received contents as the outgoing entity-body. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the cache MAY combine the contents stored in the cache entry with the new contents received in the response | N/A for cache-less client; range-combining permitted only on exact ETag/Last-Modified match. |
| `13.4-13.13-25` | MUST | When constructing a response from a cache entry after validation, stored Warning headers with warn-code 1xx must be deleted from both the cache entry and the forwarded response. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | any stored Warning headers with warn-code 1xx (see section 14.46) MUST be deleted from the cache entry and the forwarded response. | N/A for cache-less client; a client-side cache must purge 1xx Warning headers on revalidation. |
| `13.4-13.13-26` | MUST | Stored Warning headers with warn-code 2xx must be retained in the cache entry and the forwarded response. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | any stored Warning headers with warn-code 2xx MUST be retained in the cache entry and the forwarded response. | N/A for cache-less client; a client-side cache must keep 2xx Warning headers across revalidation. |
| `13.4-13.13-27` | MUST | Any end-to-end headers provided in the 304 or 206 response must replace the corresponding headers from the cache entry in the constructed response. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | any end-to-end headers provided in the 304 or 206 response MUST replace the corresponding headers from the cache entry. | N/A for cache-less client; on revalidation, incoming end-to-end headers win over stored ones. |
| `13.4-13.13-28` | MUST | Unless it removes the entry, the cache must also update the stored entry's end-to-end headers with the corresponding headers from the incoming response (Warning rules excepted). <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | it MUST also replace the end-to-end headers stored with the cache entry with corresponding headers received in the incoming response | N/A for cache-less client; header updates from 304/206 must be written back into the stored entry, not just the forwarded copy. |
| `13.4-13.13-29` | MUST | If a header field-name in the incoming response matches more than one header in the cache entry, all such old headers must be replaced. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | If a header field-name in the incoming response matches more than one header in the cache entry, all such old headers MUST be replaced. | N/A for cache-less client; multi-valued stored headers are replaced as a set, not merged one-for-one. |
| `13.4-13.13-30` | informative | A 304 or 206 response can update headers of a previous response but cannot entirely delete a header the origin server previously provided. <br>_(cache,origin [not-client])_ | This rule does not allow an origin server to use a 304 (Not Modified) or a 206 (Partial Content) response to entirely delete a header | N/A for cache-less client; header-merge logic must never drop a stored header merely because the 304/206 omitted it. |
| `13.4-13.13-31` | MAY | A cache may combine a newly received subrange with a stored set of subranges only if both the incoming response and cache entry have a cache validator and the two validators match under the strong comparison function. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the cache MAY combine the new subrange with the existing set if both the following conditions are met | N/A for cache-less client; range assembly requires validators on both sides matching by strong comparison (13.3.3). |
| `13.4-13.13-32` | MUST | If either range-combining condition is not met, the cache must use only the most recent partial response, chosen by Date values (using the incoming response on equal or missing dates). <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the cache MUST use only the most recent partial response | N/A for cache-less client; on validator mismatch keep only the newest partial response by Date. |
| `13.4-13.13-33` | MUST | In that case the cache must discard the other (older) partial information. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | and MUST discard the other partial information | N/A for cache-less client; older partial content must be evicted, never silently retained. |
| `13.4-13.13-34` | SHOULD | A server should use the Vary header field to inform caches which request-header fields were used to select among multiple representations of a negotiated cacheable response. <br>_(server,origin [not-client])_ | A server SHOULD use the Vary header field to inform a cache of what request-header fields were used to select among multiple representations | No client obligation; server-side rule. |
| `13.4-13.13-35` | MUST NOT | A cache must not use an entry containing a Vary header to construct a response unless all selecting request-headers in the new request match the stored request-headers of the original request. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the cache MUST NOT use such a cache entry to construct a response to the new request unless all of the selecting request-headers present in the new request match | N/A for cache-less client; a client-side cache must key Vary entries on the named selecting request-headers. |
| `13.4-13.13-36` | informative | Selecting request-headers match if and only if those in the first request can be transformed to those in the second by adding/removing LWS where the BNF allows and/or combining same-named header fields per section 4.2. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | can be transformed to the selecting request-headers in the second request by adding or removing linear white space (LWS) at places where this is allowed by the corresponding BNF | N/A for cache-less client; Vary matching must normalize LWS and same-name header combining before comparing. |
| `13.4-13.13-37` | informative | A Vary field-value of "*" always fails to match; such responses can only be properly interpreted by the origin server. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | A Vary header field-value of "*" always fails to match | N/A for cache-less client; Vary: * entries must never be served from cache without origin involvement. |
| `13.4-13.13-38` | MUST NOT | If selecting request-headers do not match, the cache must not use the cached entry unless it first relays the request to the origin as a conditional request and gets 304 with an entity tag or Content-Location identifying the entity to use. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the cache MUST NOT use a cached entry to satisfy the request unless it first relays the new request to the origin server in a conditional request | N/A for cache-less client; Vary mismatch requires origin revalidation before any cached entity may be served. |
| `13.4-13.13-39` | SHOULD | If an entity tag was assigned to a cached representation, the forwarded (revalidating) request should be conditional and include entity tags from all cache entries for the resource in an If-None-Match header. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the forwarded request SHOULD be conditional and include the entity tags in an If-None-Match header field from all its cache entries for the resource. | N/A for cache-less client; a revalidating client-side cache should send If-None-Match listing every stored ETag for the resource. |
| `13.4-13.13-40` | SHOULD | If the entity-tag of the new response matches an existing entry, the new response should be used to update the header fields of that existing entry. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the new response SHOULD be used to update the header fields of the existing entry | N/A for cache-less client; on ETag match during revalidation, refresh the matched entry's headers. |
| `13.4-13.13-41` | MUST | The result of that update (the selected, updated entry) must be returned to the requesting client. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | and the result MUST be returned to the client | N/A for cache-less client; the updated matched entity, not the raw 304, is what gets delivered to the caller. |
| `13.4-13.13-42` | SHOULD NOT | An entity-tag belonging to a cache entry holding only partial content should not be included in If-None-Match unless the request is for a range fully satisfied by that entry. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | its entity-tag SHOULD NOT be included in the If-None-Match header field unless the request is for a range that would be fully satisfied by that entry. | N/A for cache-less client; exclude partial-content ETags from If-None-Match unless the entry covers the requested range. |
| `13.4-13.13-43` | SHOULD NOT | If a successful response's Content-Location matches an existing entry for the same Request-URI but its entity-tag differs and its Date is newer, the existing entry should not be returned for future requests. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the existing entry SHOULD NOT be returned in response to future requests | N/A for cache-less client; a superseded variant (same Content-Location, new ETag, newer Date) must stop being served. |
| `13.4-13.13-44` | SHOULD | That superseded existing entry should also be deleted from the cache. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | and SHOULD be deleted from the cache | N/A for cache-less client; evict superseded variants rather than merely skipping them. |
| `13.4-13.13-45` | SHOULD | A non-shared cache is accessible only to a single user, and that accessibility should be enforced by appropriate security mechanisms; all other caches are shared. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | Accessibility in this case SHOULD be enforced by appropriate security mechanisms. | N/A for cache-less client; a private client-side cache must be isolated per user to qualify as non-shared. |
| `13.4-13.13-46` | MAY | A cache that receives an incomplete response (e.g. fewer body bytes than Content-Length declared) may store it. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | A cache that receives an incomplete response (for example, with fewer bytes of data than specified in a Content-Length header) MAY store the response. | N/A for cache-less client; the client itself must still surface truncation (short Content-Length reads) as an error to callers. |
| `13.4-13.13-47` | MUST | If it stores an incomplete response, the cache must treat it as a partial response. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | However, the cache MUST treat this as a partial response. | N/A for cache-less client; stored truncated responses carry partial status, never full-response status. |
| `13.4-13.13-48` | MAY | Partial responses may be combined per section 13.5.4; the result might be full or still partial. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | Partial responses MAY be combined as described in section 13.5.4 | N/A for cache-less client; combining truncated responses follows the same validator rules as byte ranges. |
| `13.4-13.13-49` | MUST NOT | A cache must not return a partial response to a client without explicitly marking it as partial using the 206 status code. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | A cache MUST NOT return a partial response to a client without explicitly marking it as such, using the 206 (Partial Content) status code. | N/A for cache-less client; a client-side cache must relabel any partial delivery as 206. |
| `13.4-13.13-50` | MUST NOT | A cache must not return a partial response using status code 200 (OK). <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | A cache MUST NOT return a partial response using a status code of 200 (OK). | N/A for cache-less client; never present incomplete cached content as a complete 200 response. |
| `13.4-13.13-51` | MAY | If a cache receives a 5xx response while revalidating an entry, it may either forward the 5xx to the requesting client or act as if the server failed to respond. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | it MAY either forward this response to the requesting client, or act as if the server failed to respond | N/A for cache-less client; be aware intermediaries may mask origin 5xx by serving stale content. |
| `13.4-13.13-52` | MAY | When acting as if the server failed, the cache may return a previously received response unless the cached entry includes the must-revalidate cache-control directive. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | it MAY return a previously received response unless the cached entry includes the "must-revalidate" cache-control directive | N/A for cache-less client; a client-side cache serving stale on 5xx must honor must-revalidate as an absolute bar. |
| `13.4-13.13-53` | SHOULD NOT | Unless the origin explicitly prohibits caching of its responses, applying GET and HEAD to any resource should not have side effects that would cause erroneous behavior when the responses are served from cache. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | the application of GET and HEAD methods to any resources SHOULD NOT have side effects that would lead to erroneous behavior if these responses are taken from a cache | No client-library obligation; resource-design rule for origin applications. |
| `13.4-13.13-54` | MAY | GET/HEAD may still have side effects, but a cache is not required to consider such side effects in its caching decisions. <br>_(origin,cache [not-client])_ | They MAY still have side effects, but a cache is not required to consider such side effects in its caching decisions. | No client obligation; callers cannot rely on caches noticing GET/HEAD side effects. |
| `13.4-13.13-55` | informative | Caches are always expected to observe an origin server's explicit restrictions on caching. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | Caches are always expected to observe an origin server's explicit restrictions on caching. | N/A for cache-less client; any future client-side cache must honor explicit origin cache-control restrictions unconditionally. |
| `13.4-13.13-56` | MUST NOT | Caches must not treat responses to query URIs (containing "?" in the rel_path) as fresh unless the server provides an explicit expiration time. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | caches MUST NOT treat responses to such URIs as fresh unless the server provides an explicit expiration time | N/A for cache-less client; a client-side cache must deny heuristic freshness for query-string URIs. |
| `13.4-13.13-57` | SHOULD NOT | Responses from HTTP/1.0 servers for query URIs should not be taken from a cache. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | responses from HTTP/1.0 servers for such URIs SHOULD NOT be taken from a cache | N/A for cache-less client; a client-side cache should bypass cache for HTTP/1.0-origin query-URI responses. |
| `13.4-13.13-58` | MUST | The methods PUT, DELETE, and POST must cause a cache to invalidate the entity referred to by the Request-URI, or by the Location or Content-Location headers if present. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | Some HTTP methods MUST cause a cache to invalidate an entity. | N/A for cache-less client; a client-side cache must invalidate (remove or mark for mandatory revalidation) on PUT/DELETE/POST. |
| `13.4-13.13-59` | MUST | To prevent denial-of-service, invalidation based on a URI in a Location or Content-Location header must only be performed if that URI's host part matches the Request-URI's host. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | an invalidation based on the URI in a Location or Content-Location header MUST only be performed if the host part is the same as in the Request-URI | N/A for cache-less client; header-driven invalidation must be gated on same-host comparison against the Request-URI. |
| `13.4-13.13-60` | SHOULD | A cache that passes through requests for methods it does not understand should invalidate any entities referred to by the Request-URI. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | A cache that passes through requests for methods it does not understand SHOULD invalidate any entities referred to by the Request-URI. | N/A for cache-less client; unknown methods routed through a client-side cache should invalidate the target URI's entries. |
| `13.4-13.13-61` | MUST | All methods that might be expected to modify origin resources (currently all methods except GET and HEAD) must be written through to the origin server. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | All methods that might be expected to cause modifications to the origin server's resources MUST be written through to the origin server. | N/A for cache-less client (all requests already go to the wire); a client-side cache must never answer non-GET/HEAD from cache. |
| `13.4-13.13-62` | MUST NOT | A cache must not reply to such a (write-through) request before having transmitted it to the inbound server and received a corresponding response. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | A cache MUST NOT reply to such a request from a client before having transmitted the request to the inbound server | N/A for cache-less client; no synthesized responses for modifying methods before an origin round trip completes. |
| `13.4-13.13-63` | informative | Write-through does not prevent a proxy cache from sending a 100 (Continue) response before the inbound server has sent its final reply. <br>_(proxy,cache [not-client])_ | This does not prevent a proxy cache from sending a 100 (Continue) response before the inbound server has sent its final reply. | No client obligation; client's 100-continue handling must tolerate a proxy-originated 100 preceding the origin's final response. |
| `13.4-13.13-64` | informative | Write-back (copy-back) caching is not allowed in HTTP/1.1. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | The alternative (known as "write-back" or "copy-back" caching) is not allowed in HTTP/1.1 | N/A for cache-less client; never implement deferred write-back semantics in any client-side cache. |
| `13.4-13.13-65` | SHOULD | If a new cacheable response arrives while existing responses for the same resource are cached, the cache should use the new response to reply to the current request. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the cache SHOULD use the new response to reply to the current request | N/A for cache-less client; freshest response wins for the in-flight request in a client-side cache. |
| `13.4-13.13-66` | MAY | The cache may insert the new response into storage and may, if it meets all other requirements, use it to answer future requests that would previously have returned the old response. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | It MAY insert it into cache storage and MAY, if it meets all other requirements, use it to respond to any future requests | N/A for cache-less client; when inserting a replacement response, apply the 13.5.3 header-combining rules. |
| `13.4-13.13-67` | informative | A new response with an older Date header value than existing cached responses is not cacheable. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | a new response that has an older Date header value than existing cached responses is not cacheable | N/A for cache-less client; a client-side cache must reject replacement candidates whose Date is older than what is stored. |
| `13.4-13.13-68` | SHOULD NOT | History mechanisms (e.g. Back buttons) should not try to show a semantically transparent view of the current state of a resource; they show what the user saw at retrieval time. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | history mechanisms SHOULD NOT try to show a semantically transparent view of the current state of a resource | Vacuously satisfied — the library has no history mechanism; if one is ever added, replay the stored view rather than revalidating. |
| `13.4-13.13-69` | informative | By default, an expiration time does not apply to history mechanisms. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | By default, an expiration time does not apply to history mechanisms. | Vacuously satisfied — no history mechanism; do not apply cache expiration logic to any future history feature. |
| `13.4-13.13-70` | SHOULD | If the entity is still in storage, a history mechanism should display it even if expired, unless the user has specifically configured the agent to refresh expired history documents. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | If the entity is still in storage, a history mechanism SHOULD display it even if the entity has expired, unless the user has specifically configured the agent to refresh | Vacuously satisfied — no history mechanism; a future one should show expired stored entities by default (staleness notice permitted). |

### 14.1-14.8 - Headers: Accept*, Accept-Ranges, Age, Allow, Authorization

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `14.1-14.8-1` | informative | For entity-header fields, 'sender' and 'recipient' mean client or server depending on the direction the entity travels. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | For entity-header fields, both sender and recipient refer to either the client or the server, depending on who sends and who receives the entity. | Interpret entity-header requirements (e.g. Allow) as binding the client when the client sends the entity. |
| `14.1-14.8-2` | informative | Accept request-header grammar: a #-list of media-range optionally followed by accept-params. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Accept = "Accept" ":" #( media-range [ accept-params ] ) | When emitting Accept, produce a comma-separated list of media-range [accept-params]; #-list permits an empty list. |
| `14.1-14.8-3` | informative | media-range grammar: */*, type/*, or type/subtype, each optionally followed by parameters. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | media-range = ( "*/*" \| ( type "/" "*" ) \| ( type "/" subtype ) ) *( ";" parameter ) | Validate emitted media-ranges as one of the three forms with optional ;parameter list; type/subtype are tokens. |
| `14.1-14.8-4` | informative | accept-params grammar: begins with ;q=qvalue then zero or more accept-extensions. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | accept-params = ";" "q" "=" qvalue *( accept-extension ) | If a quality factor is emitted it must be spelled ;q=qvalue and precede any accept-extension. |
| `14.1-14.8-5` | informative | accept-extension grammar: ;token optionally =token or =quoted-string. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | accept-extension = ";" token [ "=" ( token \| quoted-string ) ] | Extensions after q must be token[=(token\|quoted-string)]; quote values that are not tokens. |
| `14.1-14.8-6` | informative | The asterisk groups media types into ranges: */* means all types, type/* means all subtypes of a type. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | The asterisk "*" character is used to group media types into ranges, with "*/*" indicating all media types and "type/*" indicating all subtypes of that type. | Emit */* or type/* only when the client genuinely accepts the whole range. |
| `14.1-14.8-7` | MAY | A media-range may include media type parameters applicable to that range. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The media-range MAY include media type parameters that are applicable to that range. | Allow (but do not require) media-type parameters inside emitted media-ranges. |
| `14.1-14.8-8` | MAY | Each media-range may be followed by one or more accept-params starting with the q parameter. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Each media-range MAY be followed by one or more accept-params, beginning with the "q" parameter for indicating a relative quality factor. | Support optional per-range quality factors; q must come first among accept-params. |
| `14.1-14.8-9` | informative | The first q parameter is the delimiter between media-range parameters and accept-params. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | The first "q" parameter (if any) separates the media-range parameter(s) from the accept-params. | When building Accept, place any media-type parameters before q; everything after the first q is accept-extension territory. |
| `14.1-14.8-10` | informative | Quality factors express relative preference on the qvalue scale 0 to 1. <br>_(client,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | Quality factors allow the user or user agent to indicate the relative degree of preference for that media-range, using the qvalue scale from 0 to 1 (section 3.9). | Clamp/validate emitted qvalues to the 0..1 qvalue syntax of section 3.9 (max 3 decimal digits). |
| `14.1-14.8-11` | informative | A media-range without an explicit q parameter has quality 1 by default. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | The default value is q=1. | Omit q for full-preference ranges; treat absent q as 1.0 in any client-side Accept handling. |
| `14.1-14.8-12` | SHOULD | An Accept value like 'audio/*; q=0.2, audio/basic' should be interpreted as preferring the specific type, with the range as marked-down fallback. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | SHOULD be interpreted as "I prefer audio/basic, but send me any audio type if it is the best available after an 80% mark-down in quality." | Server-side interpretation rule; no client code obligation beyond emitting qvalues consistent with intent. |
| `14.1-14.8-13` | informative | Absence of an Accept header means the client accepts all media types. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | If no Accept header field is present, then it is assumed that the client accepts all media types. | Do not auto-inject a default Accept header; omission already means accept-everything, so the caller must opt in. |
| `14.1-14.8-14` | SHOULD | If the server cannot satisfy the combined Accept field it should respond 406. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | if the server cannot send a response which is acceptable according to the combined Accept field value, then the server SHOULD send a 406 (not acceptable) response. | No emit-side action; surface 406 responses to the caller as a normal status (not a transport error) when Accept was sent. |
| `14.1-14.8-15` | informative | More specific media ranges/types override less specific ones; the most specific reference wins. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If more than one media range applies to a given type, the most specific reference has precedence. | Server matching rule; client only needs it if it implements content-negotiation evaluation, which a request library does not. |
| `14.1-14.8-16` | informative | The quality factor for a type is that of the highest-precedence matching media range. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | The media type quality factor associated with a given type is determined by finding the media range with the highest precedence which matches that type. | Server-side computation; no client obligation. |
