| `14.1-14.8-17` | informative | Any default set of Accept quality values in a user agent ought to be user-configurable (unless a closed system). <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | unless the user agent is a closed system which cannot interact with other rendering agents, this default set ought to be configurable by the user. | If the library ever ships default Accept qvalues, expose them as caller configuration rather than hardcoding. |
| `14.1-14.8-18` | informative | Accept-Charset grammar: 1#-list of (charset or *) each with optional ;q=qvalue. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Accept-Charset = "Accept-Charset" ":" 1#( ( charset \| "*" )[ ";" "q" "=" qvalue ] ) | Emit at least one element (1# forbids empty value); each element is charset or * with optional ;q=qvalue. |
| `14.1-14.8-19` | MAY | Each charset may carry an associated quality value expressing user preference. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Each charset MAY be given an associated quality value which represents the user's preference for that charset. | Support optional per-charset qvalues in Accept-Charset emission. |
| `14.1-14.8-20` | informative | A charset without explicit q has quality 1 by default. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | The default value is q=1. | Treat/emit listed charsets without q as q=1. |
| `14.1-14.8-21` | informative | '*' in Accept-Charset matches every charset (including ISO-8859-1) not otherwise mentioned in the field. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | The special value "*", if present in the Accept-Charset field, matches every character set (including ISO-8859-1) which is not mentioned elsewhere in the Accept-Charset field. | Only emit * when the client truly accepts arbitrary charsets; it covers everything unlisted. |
| `14.1-14.8-22` | informative | Without '*', unmentioned charsets get q=0 except ISO-8859-1, which implicitly gets q=1. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | all character sets not explicitly mentioned get a quality value of 0, except for ISO-8859-1, which gets a quality value of 1 if not explicitly mentioned. | When emitting Accept-Charset without *, know the server may still send ISO-8859-1; list iso-8859-1;q=0 explicitly to refuse it. |
| `14.1-14.8-23` | informative | Absence of Accept-Charset means any character set is acceptable. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | If no Accept-Charset header is present, the default is that any character set is acceptable. | Do not auto-inject Accept-Charset; omission means the client accepts all charsets. |
| `14.1-14.8-24` | SHOULD | If no acceptable charset can be sent the server should reply 406, though sending an unacceptable response is allowed. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | the server SHOULD send an error response with the 406 (not acceptable) status code, though the sending of an unacceptable response is also allowed. | Client must tolerate both outcomes: a 406, or a response in a charset it declared unacceptable. |
| `14.1-14.8-25` | informative | Accept-Encoding grammar: 1#-list of codings each with optional ;q=qvalue. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Accept-Encoding = "Accept-Encoding" ":" 1#( codings [ ";" "q" "=" qvalue ] ) | Emit codings with optional ;q=qvalue; note the RFC's own examples include an empty 'Accept-Encoding:' value with defined semantics (fact 31). |
| `14.1-14.8-26` | informative | codings grammar: a content-coding token or '*'. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | codings = ( content-coding \| "*" ) | Each Accept-Encoding element is a content-coding token or the wildcard *. |
| `14.1-14.8-27` | informative | A listed content-coding is acceptable unless its qvalue is 0 (q=0 means not acceptable). <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | If the content-coding is one of the content-codings listed in the Accept-Encoding field, then it is acceptable, unless it is accompanied by a qvalue of 0. | Only list codings the client's decoder supports; use ;q=0 to explicitly refuse a coding. |
| `14.1-14.8-28` | informative | '*' in Accept-Encoding matches any available content-coding not explicitly listed. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | The special "*" symbol in an Accept-Encoding field matches any available content-coding not explicitly listed in the header field. | Emit * only if the client can decode arbitrary codings; explicit listings override it. |
| `14.1-14.8-29` | informative | Among multiple acceptable codings, the one with the highest non-zero qvalue is preferred. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | If multiple content-codings are acceptable, then the acceptable content-coding with the highest non-zero qvalue is preferred. | Order preferences via qvalues; the server picks the highest non-zero q the client sent. |
| `14.1-14.8-30` | informative | identity is always acceptable unless explicitly refused by identity;q=0, or by *;q=0 without identity listed. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | The "identity" content-coding is always acceptable, unless specifically refused because the Accept-Encoding field includes "identity;q=0", or because the field includes "*;q=0" and does not explicitly include the "identity" content-coding. | Even when sending e.g. 'Accept-Encoding: gzip', the client must still accept identity (uncoded) responses; only identity;q=0 or *;q=0 refuses it. |
| `14.1-14.8-31` | informative | An empty Accept-Encoding field-value means only the identity encoding is acceptable. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | If the Accept-Encoding field-value is empty, then only the "identity" encoding is acceptable. | To force uncompressed responses, the client can send 'Accept-Encoding:' with an empty value; parser/emitter must permit the empty value. |
| `14.1-14.8-32` | SHOULD | If no acceptable content-coding exists per Accept-Encoding, the server should respond 406. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | if the server cannot send a response which is acceptable according to the Accept-Encoding header, then the server SHOULD send an error response with the 406 (Not Acceptable) status code. | Client must handle 406 as a possible answer whenever it sends a restrictive Accept-Encoding. |
| `14.1-14.8-33` | MAY | Without Accept-Encoding in the request, the server may assume the client accepts any content-coding. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | If no Accept-Encoding field is present in a request, the server MAY assume that the client will accept any content coding. | Critical: if the client omits Accept-Encoding it must be prepared to receive ANY coding; a client that cannot decode gzip/compress should send an explicit Accept-Encoding (e.g. identity). |
| `14.1-14.8-34` | SHOULD | When Accept-Encoding is absent and identity is available, the server should use identity. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | if "identity" is one of the available content-codings, then the server SHOULD use the "identity" content-coding, unless it has additional information that a different content-coding is meaningful to the client. | Server obligation; client may rely on it in practice but must not depend on it (see fact 33). |
| `14.1-14.8-35` | informative | If identity is unavailable and no Accept-Encoding was sent, codings HTTP/1.0 clients understand (gzip, compress) are preferred. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | content-codings commonly understood by HTTP/1.0 clients (i.e., "gzip" and "compress") are preferred | Server heuristic in a Note; no client action. |
| `14.1-14.8-36` | informative | qvalues are not permitted with the x-gzip or x-compress coding names. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | qvalues will not work and are not permitted with x-gzip or x-compress. | Never attach ;q= to x-gzip or x-compress in an emitted Accept-Encoding. |
| `14.1-14.8-37` | informative | Accept-Language grammar: 1#-list of language-range each with optional ;q=qvalue. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Accept-Language = "Accept-Language" ":" 1#( language-range [ ";" "q" "=" qvalue ] ) | Emit at least one language-range (1# forbids empty); optional ;q=qvalue per range. |
| `14.1-14.8-38` | informative | language-range grammar: 1-8 ALPHA subtags joined by '-', or '*'. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | language-range = ( ( 1*8ALPHA *( "-" 1*8ALPHA ) ) \| "*" ) | Validate emitted language ranges: alphabetic subtags of 1-8 chars separated by hyphens, or the * wildcard. |
| `14.1-14.8-39` | MAY | Each language-range may carry a quality value estimating the user's preference. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Each language-range MAY be given an associated quality value which represents an estimate of the user's preference for the languages specified by that range. | Support optional per-range qvalues in Accept-Language emission. |
| `14.1-14.8-40` | informative | A language-range without explicit q defaults to q=1. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | The quality value defaults to "q=1". | Treat/emit ranges without q as full preference. |
| `14.1-14.8-41` | informative | A language-range matches a tag by exact equality or as a prefix ending at a '-' boundary. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A language-range matches a language-tag if it exactly equals the tag, or if it exactly equals a prefix of the tag such that the first tag character following the prefix is "-". | When choosing what to emit, know 'en' matches en-gb but 'en-gb' does not match 'en'; add broader prefixes for fallback. |
| `14.1-14.8-42` | informative | '*' in Accept-Language matches every tag not matched by any other listed range. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | The special range "*", if present in the Accept-Language field, matches every tag not matched by any other range present in the Accept-Language field. | Emit * only as a catch-all; explicit ranges take the match away from it. |
| `14.1-14.8-43` | informative | The quality factor for a tag is the qvalue of the longest matching language-range in the field. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | The language quality factor assigned to a language-tag by the Accept-Language field is the quality value of the longest language-range in the field that matches the language-tag. | Server-side computation; no client code obligation. |
| `14.1-14.8-44` | informative | A tag matched by no language-range gets quality factor 0. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If no language-range in the field matches the tag, the language quality factor assigned is 0. | Server-side computation; no client code obligation. |
| `14.1-14.8-45` | SHOULD | Without Accept-Language, the server should assume all languages are equally acceptable. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | If no Accept-Language header is present in the request, the server SHOULD assume that all languages are equally acceptable. | Do not auto-inject Accept-Language; omission already means all languages acceptable. |
| `14.1-14.8-46` | informative | When Accept-Language is present, every language with quality factor greater than 0 is acceptable. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If an Accept-Language header is present, then all languages which are assigned a quality factor greater than 0 are acceptable. | Server-side interpretation; no client obligation. |
| `14.1-14.8-47` | RECOMMENDED | Client applications are recommended to let the user choose linguistic preference. <br>_(client,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | it is recommended that client applications make the choice of linguistic preference available to the user. | Expose Accept-Language as caller-supplied configuration, never a library-chosen value. |
| `14.1-14.8-48` | MUST NOT | If linguistic choice is not made available to the user, Accept-Language must not be sent. <br>_(client,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | If the choice is not made available, then the Accept-Language header field MUST NOT be given in the request. | Checklist: the library must never synthesize a default Accept-Language header; only send it when the caller explicitly provides one. |
| `14.1-14.8-49` | informative | Accept-Ranges response-header grammar: acceptable-ranges value. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | Accept-Ranges = "Accept-Ranges" ":" acceptable-ranges | Parse Accept-Ranges from responses and expose it to callers deciding on range requests. |
| `14.1-14.8-50` | informative | acceptable-ranges grammar: a 1#-list of range-units or the literal 'none'. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | acceptable-ranges = 1#range-unit \| "none" | Parser must accept either a non-empty list of range-unit tokens or the single token none. |
| `14.1-14.8-51` | MAY | Origin servers accepting byte ranges may send Accept-Ranges: bytes but are not required to. <br>_(origin,server [not-client])_ | Origin servers that accept byte-range requests MAY send Accept-Ranges: bytes but are not required to do so. | Origin obligation; client takeaway is only that absence of the header proves nothing (see fact 52). |
| `14.1-14.8-52` | MAY | Clients may issue byte-range requests without having seen Accept-Ranges for the resource. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Clients MAY generate byte-range requests without having received this header for the resource involved. | Do not gate Range request emission on prior receipt of Accept-Ranges: bytes; speculative range requests are legal. |
| `14.1-14.8-53` | MAY | Servers rejecting all range requests for a resource may send Accept-Ranges: none to advise against range requests. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | Servers that do not accept any kind of range request for a resource MAY send Accept-Ranges: none to advise the client not to attempt a range request. | Treat Accept-Ranges: none as advisory: prefer not to attempt range requests against that resource. |
| `14.1-14.8-54` | informative | Age response-header grammar: age-value which is delta-seconds. <br>_(server,cache,client [CLIENT])_ | Age = "Age" ":" age-value age-value = delta-seconds | Parse Age as a delta-seconds value on received responses. |
| `14.1-14.8-55` | informative | Age values are non-negative decimal integers counting seconds. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Age values are non-negative decimal integers, representing time in seconds. | Age parser: decimal digits only, no sign, no fraction; reject or ignore malformed values. |
| `14.1-14.8-56` | MUST | On overflow of its representable range or its age arithmetic, a cache must transmit Age: 2147483648 (2^31). <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | or if any of its age calculations overflows, it MUST transmit an Age header with a value of 2147483648 (2^31). | Binds caches only (N/A while the client has no cache), but the client's Age parser must accept values >= 2^31 without overflow (use uint32/int64). |
| `14.1-14.8-57` | MUST | An HTTP/1.1 server with a cache must include Age in every response served from its own cache. <br>_(server,cache [not-client])_ | An HTTP/1.1 server that includes a cache MUST include an Age header field in every response generated from its own cache. | Server/cache obligation; client may use presence of Age as a cached-response signal but has no duty. |
| `14.1-14.8-58` | SHOULD | Caches should use an arithmetic type with at least 31 bits of range for age values. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | Caches SHOULD use an arithmetic type of at least 31 bits of range. | Cache obligation; if the client ever exposes parsed Age, use >= 31-bit unsigned arithmetic anyway. |
| `14.1-14.8-59` | MUST | A 405 (Method Not Allowed) response must contain an Allow header field. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | An Allow header field MUST be present in a 405 (Method Not Allowed) response. | Server obligation; client may read Allow on 405 but must tolerate non-conforming servers that omit it. |
| `14.1-14.8-60` | informative | Allow entity-header grammar: a #-list of Method tokens (empty list permitted). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Allow = "Allow" ":" #Method | Parse Allow as comma-separated method tokens; #Method allows an empty value; also used when the client emits Allow on PUT. |
| `14.1-14.8-61` | SHOULD | The client should follow the method indications given by the Allow header value. <br>_(client,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | the indications given by the Allow header field value SHOULD be followed. | Expose Allow to callers; a well-behaved client avoids retrying with methods not listed in a received Allow. |
| `14.1-14.8-62` | informative | The allowed-method set is defined by the origin server at the time of each request. <br>_(origin,client [CLIENT])_ | The actual set of allowed methods is defined by the origin server at the time of each request. | Do not cache Allow values across requests as authoritative; they are a per-response snapshot. |
| `14.1-14.8-63` | MAY | A client may include Allow in a PUT request to recommend methods for the new or modified resource. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The Allow header field MAY be provided with a PUT request to recommend the methods to be supported by the new or modified resource. | Permit callers to set Allow on outgoing PUT requests; never reject it as a response-only header. |
| `14.1-14.8-64` | SHOULD | The server need not honor a PUT's Allow and should respond with an Allow listing the actually supported methods. <br>_(server,origin [not-client])_ | The server is not required to support these methods and SHOULD include an Allow header in the response giving the actual supported methods. | Server obligation; client should treat the response's Allow (not its own request's) as authoritative. |
| `14.1-14.8-65` | MUST NOT | A proxy must not modify the Allow header even if it does not understand all listed methods. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A proxy MUST NOT modify the Allow header field even if it does not understand all the methods specified | Proxy obligation; no client action (relevant only if this codebase ever adds a proxy mode). |
| `14.1-14.8-66` | informative | A user agent authenticates itself by including an Authorization request-header, usually but not necessarily after a 401. <br>_(client,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | A user agent that wishes to authenticate itself with a server--usually, but not necessarily, after receiving a 401 response--does so by including an Authorization request-header field with the request. | Support sending Authorization on any request, including preemptively (before any 401 is seen). |
| `14.1-14.8-67` | informative | Authorization request-header grammar: credentials. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Authorization = "Authorization" ":" credentials | Emit Authorization as scheme + credentials per RFC 2617; the field value is opaque credentials to this layer. |
| `14.1-14.8-68` | SHOULD | Once a request is authenticated for a realm, the same credentials should be valid for other requests in that realm. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | If a request is authenticated and a realm specified, the same credentials SHOULD be valid for all other requests within this realm | A client may reuse credentials preemptively for subsequent requests within the same realm (unless the scheme is challenge-varying). |
| `14.1-14.8-69` | MUST NOT | A shared cache must not reuse a response to a request that carried Authorization for any other request, absent listed exceptions. <br>_(cache,proxy [not-client])_ | it MUST NOT return the corresponding response as a reply to any other request, unless one of the following specific exceptions holds | Shared-cache obligation; N/A for this client (no shared cache); becomes binding only if a caching layer is added. |
| `14.1-14.8-70` | MAY | Exception 1: with the s-maxage directive a cache may reuse the response for a subsequent request. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | If the response includes the "s-maxage" cache-control directive, the cache MAY use that response in replying to a subsequent request. | Cache-only rule; no client obligation. |
| `14.1-14.8-71` | MUST | Under exception 1, once the s-maxage age has passed, a proxy cache must revalidate with the origin using the new request's headers. <br>_(proxy,cache [not-client])_ | But (if the specified maximum age has passed) a proxy cache MUST first revalidate it with the origin server, using the request-headers from the new request | Proxy-cache-only rule; no client obligation. |
| `14.1-14.8-72` | MUST | A response with s-maxage=0 must always be revalidated by the proxy before reuse. <br>_(proxy,cache [not-client])_ | If the response includes "s-maxage=0", the proxy MUST always revalidate it before re-using it. | Proxy-cache-only rule; no client obligation. |
| `14.1-14.8-73` | MAY | Exception 2: with the must-revalidate directive a cache may reuse the response for a subsequent request. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | If the response includes the "must-revalidate" cache-control directive, the cache MAY use that response in replying to a subsequent request. | Cache-only rule; no client obligation. |
| `14.1-14.8-74` | MUST | Under exception 2, if the response is stale, all caches must first revalidate with the origin using the new request's headers. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | But if the response is stale, all caches MUST first revalidate it with the origin server, using the request-headers from the new request | Cache-only rule; no client obligation. |
| `14.1-14.8-75` | MAY | Exception 3: with the public directive the response may be returned for any subsequent request. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | If the response includes the "public" cache-control directive, it MAY be returned in reply to any subsequent request. | Cache-only rule; no client obligation. |

### 14.9 - Header: Cache-Control (all directives)

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `14.9-1` | MUST | Cache-Control directives must be obeyed by every caching mechanism along the request/response chain. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | The Cache-Control general-header field is used to specify directives that MUST be obeyed by all caching mechanisms along the request/response chain. | No action for a cache-less client; if a client-side cache is ever added, it must honor every directive it receives. |
| `14.9-2` | MUST | Proxies and gateways must forward cache directives unchanged regardless of whether they understand them. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | Cache directives MUST be passed through by a proxy or gateway application, regardless of their significance to that application | Proxy/gateway-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.9-3` | informative | Grammar: the Cache-Control header value is a comma-separated list of one or more cache-directives (1#cache-directive). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Cache-Control = "Cache-Control" ":" 1#cache-directive | Emit Cache-Control as a comma-separated directive list with at least one directive; parse received values as a #-list (comma-separated, optional LWS). |
| `14.9-4` | informative | Grammar: a cache-directive is either a cache-request-directive or a cache-response-directive. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | cache-directive = cache-request-directive \| cache-response-directive | Keep request-directive and response-directive vocabularies distinct; only request directives are valid for the client to emit. |
| `14.9-5` | informative | Grammar: request directives are no-cache, no-store, max-age=delta-seconds, max-stale[=delta-seconds], min-fresh=delta-seconds, no-transform, only-if-cached, or a cache-extension. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | "no-cache" \| "no-store" \| "max-age" "=" delta-seconds \| "max-stale" [ "=" delta-seconds ] \| "min-fresh" "=" delta-seconds \| "no-transform" \| "only-if-cached" \| cache-extension | Client emit path must produce only these forms: max-age/min-fresh require a value, max-stale value is optional, the rest are bare tokens. |
| `14.9-6` | informative | Grammar: response directives are public, private[="1#field-name"], no-cache[="1#field-name"], no-store, no-transform, must-revalidate, proxy-revalidate, max-age=delta-seconds, s-maxage=delta-seconds, or a cache-extension. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | "public" \| "private" [ "=" <"> 1#field-name <"> ] \| "no-cache" [ "=" <"> 1#field-name <"> ] \| "no-store" \| "no-transform" \| "must-revalidate" \| "proxy-revalidate" \| "max-age" "=" delta-seconds \| "s-maxage" "=" delta-seconds \| cache-extension | Response parser must accept these forms, including the quoted-string field-name list argument on private and no-cache. |
| `14.9-7` | informative | Grammar: a cache-extension is a token with an optional value that is either a token or a quoted-string. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | cache-extension = token [ "=" ( token \| quoted-string ) ] | Parser must tolerate unknown token[=token\|quoted-string] directives without error; emitter may produce them. |
| `14.9-8` | informative | A directive without a field-name parameter applies to the whole request/response; with a field-name parameter it applies only to the named fields. <br>_(cache [CLIENT])_ | When a directive appears without any 1#field-name parameter, the directive applies to the entire request or response. | When parsing private/no-cache, distinguish the bare form (whole message) from the ="field,field" form (named headers only) and expose both. |
| `14.9-9` | informative | Revalidation and reload controls (request no-cache, max-age, max-stale, min-fresh, only-if-cached class) may only be imposed by a user agent. <br>_(user-agent,origin [CLIENT])_ | Controls over cache revalidation and reload; these may only be imposed by a user agent. | **VERIFY** The client/user-agent is the sole legitimate source of revalidation/reload request directives; the API should let callers set them. -- The RFC clause states these controls 'may only be imposed by a user agent.' applies_to must be ['user-agent'] only; listing 'origin' directly contradicts the exclusivity of the quoted clause. Quote is verbatim and level (informative) is correct, but the origin binding is wrong. \|\| applies_to is wrong: the RFC bullet reads 'Controls over cache revalidation and reload; these may only be imposed by a user agent.' Origin is explicitly EXCLUDED, so applies_to should be ["user-agent"] only, not ["user-agent","origin"]. (Quote itself is verbatim and level informative is correct.) |
| `14.9-10` | informative | By default a response is cacheable when the request method, request headers, and response status indicate cacheability (per section 13.4). <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | By default, a response is cacheable if the requirements of the request method, request header fields, and the response status indicate that it is cacheable. | Cache-decision default; only relevant if a client-side cache is implemented. |
| `14.9-11` | MAY | The public directive permits any cache to store the response, even one normally non-cacheable or cacheable only in a private cache. <br>_(cache,origin [not-client])_ | Indicates that the response MAY be cached by any cache, even if it would normally be non-cacheable or cacheable only within a non-shared cache. | Cache-only semantics; a cache-less client need only parse and surface the token. |
| `14.9-12` | MUST NOT | A response (or the named parts) marked private must not be stored by a shared cache. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | intended for a single user and MUST NOT be cached by a shared cache | Binds shared caches; no obligation for a client. |
| `14.9-13` | MAY | A private (non-shared) cache is permitted to cache a response marked private. <br>_(cache,user-agent [not-client])_ | A private (non-shared) cache MAY cache the response. | Applies only if the client implements a private cache; then private responses remain storable. |
| `14.9-14` | MUST NOT | Response no-cache without a field-name forbids a cache from using the response for a later request without successful revalidation with the origin. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | a cache MUST NOT use the response to satisfy a subsequent request without successful revalidation with the origin server | Cache-only; a client-side cache, if added, must revalidate before reuse. |
| `14.9-15` | MAY | Response no-cache with field-names still allows the cache to use the response for later requests, subject to other caching restrictions. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | then a cache MAY use the response to satisfy a subsequent request, subject to any other restrictions on caching | Cache-only; field-scoped no-cache does not block reuse of the rest of the response. |
| `14.9-16` | MUST NOT | The field-names listed in a response no-cache directive must not be sent in a reused response without successful revalidation with the origin. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the specified field-name(s) MUST NOT be sent in the response to a subsequent request without successful revalidation with the origin server | Cache-only; a client-side cache must strip or revalidate the named headers before reuse. |
| `14.9-17` | MAY | no-store applies to the entire message and may be sent in either a request or a response. <br>_(client,origin [CLIENT])_ | The no-store directive applies to the entire message, and MAY be sent either in a response or in a request. | Client emit path must allow no-store on requests; parser must accept it in responses. |
| `14.9-18` | MUST NOT | If no-store is sent in a request, a cache must not store any part of that request or of any response to it. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | If sent in a request, a cache MUST NOT store any part of either this request or any response to it. | Binds caches; the client just needs to be able to emit request no-store (14.9-17). |
| `14.9-19` | MUST NOT | If no-store is sent in a response, a cache must not store any part of that response or of the request that elicited it. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | If sent in a response, a cache MUST NOT store any part of either this response or the request that elicited it. | Cache-only; a client-side cache must fully skip storage of no-store responses. |
| `14.9-20` | MUST NOT | Under no-store, a cache must not intentionally store the information in non-volatile storage. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the cache MUST NOT intentionally store the information in non-volatile storage | Cache-only; if a disk-backed client cache is ever added, no-store content must never touch disk. |
| `14.9-21` | MUST | Under no-store, a cache must make a best-effort attempt to purge the information from volatile storage promptly after forwarding it. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | MUST make a best-effort attempt to remove the information from volatile storage as promptly as possible after forwarding it | Cache-only; in-memory client cache must evict no-store data as soon as it is delivered. |
| `14.9-22` | MAY | History buffers are permitted to store no-store responses as part of normal operation. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | History buffers MAY store such responses as part of their normal operation. | A client-side history/replay buffer is exempt from no-store; do not conflate it with the HTTP cache. |
| `14.9-23` | MAY | The origin server may specify an entity's expiration time using the Expires header. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | The expiration time of an entity MAY be specified by the origin server using the Expires header | Origin-side option; client freshness math (if caching) must read Expires. |
| `14.9-24` | MAY | Alternatively, expiration may be specified with a max-age directive in the response. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | it MAY be specified using the max-age directive in a response | Origin-side option; client cache (if any) must parse response max-age as an expiry source. |
| `14.9-25` | informative | With response max-age present, the cached response is stale when its current age exceeds the given value in seconds at the time of a new request. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the response is stale if its current age is greater than the age value given (in seconds) at the time of a new request for that resource | Freshness computation rule; implement in the cache layer if one is added. |
| `14.9-26` | informative | A response max-age implies the response is cacheable (as if public) unless a more restrictive directive is also present. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | The max-age directive on a response implies that the response is cacheable (i.e., "public") unless some other, more restrictive cache directive is also present. | Cache-decision rule; max-age alone makes a response storable. |
| `14.9-27` | informative | When a response has both Expires and max-age, max-age overrides Expires even if Expires is more restrictive. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the max-age directive overrides the Expires header, even if the Expires header is more restrictive | Precedence rule for freshness computation: max-age wins over Expires. |
| `14.9-28` | SHOULD | An HTTP/1.1 cache receiving a response with Expires less than or equal to Date and no Cache-Control header should treat it as non-cacheable for HTTP/1.0 compatibility. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | it SHOULD consider the response to be non-cacheable in order to retain compatibility with HTTP/1.0 servers | Cache-only compatibility rule: Expires<=Date without Cache-Control means do not cache. |
| `14.9-29` | MAY | An origin server may exploit max-age-overrides-Expires to restrict HTTP/1.1 caches while preventing pre-HTTP/1.1 caches from caching at all. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | MAY exploit the requirement that the max-age directive overrides the Expires header | Origin-side technique; no client obligation. |
| `14.9-30` | informative | For a shared cache (not a private cache), s-maxage overrides both the max-age directive and the Expires header. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | for a shared cache (but not for a private cache), the maximum age specified by this directive overrides the maximum age specified by either the max-age directive or the Expires header | Shared-cache-only precedence; a client-side (private) cache never applies s-maxage. |
| `14.9-31` | informative | s-maxage also carries proxy-revalidate semantics: a shared cache must revalidate the entry once stale before reuse. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | The s-maxage directive also implies the semantics of the proxy-revalidate directive | Shared-cache-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.9-32` | informative | A private cache always ignores the s-maxage directive. <br>_(cache,user-agent [not-client])_ | The s-maxage directive is always ignored by a private cache. | If a client-side private cache is added, it must skip s-maxage in freshness math. |
| `14.9-33` | MAY | The expiration-modifying directives max-age, min-fresh, and max-stale may be specified by the client on a request. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | These directives MAY be specified on a request: | Expose request-side max-age/min-fresh/max-stale in the client API and serialize them per the grammar. |
| `14.9-34` | informative | Request max-age means the client will accept only a response whose age is no greater than the given seconds, and (absent max-stale) not a stale one. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Indicates that the client is willing to accept a response whose age is no greater than the specified time in seconds. | Emit max-age=N on requests only when the caller intends this age bound; do not pair with stale acceptance unless max-stale also set. |
| `14.9-35` | informative | Request min-fresh means the client wants a response that will remain fresh for at least the given number of seconds beyond its current age. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | the client is willing to accept a response whose freshness lifetime is no less than its current age plus the specified time in seconds | Serialize min-fresh=N with a mandatory delta-seconds value carrying this meaning. |
| `14.9-36` | informative | Request max-stale with a value means the client accepts a response expired by no more than the given number of seconds. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | then the client is willing to accept a response that has exceeded its expiration time by no more than the specified number of seconds | Support the valued form max-stale=N meaning bounded staleness tolerance. |
| `14.9-37` | informative | Request max-stale without a value means the client accepts a stale response of any age. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If no value is assigned to max-stale, then the client is willing to accept a stale response of any age. | Support bare max-stale (no =value) as unlimited staleness tolerance; the value must be optional in the emitter. |
| `14.9-38` | MUST | A cache returning a stale response (due to max-stale or configuration) must attach a Warning 110 (Response is stale) header. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | the cache MUST attach a Warning header to the stale response, using Warning 110 (Response is stale) | Cache-only; client may surface Warning headers but has no duty here without a cache. |
| `14.9-39` | MAY | A cache may be configured to serve stale responses without validation only where that does not conflict with MUST-level validation requirements such as must-revalidate. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | A cache MAY be configured to return stale responses without validation, but only if this does not conflict with any "MUST"-level requirements concerning cache validation | Cache-only; a client cache's serve-stale knob must still yield to must-revalidate. |
| `14.9-40` | informative | When both the new request and the cached entry carry max-age, the lesser of the two values governs freshness for that request. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | then the lesser of the two values is used for determining the freshness of the cached entry for that request | Cache freshness rule: min(request max-age, response max-age). |
| `14.9-41` | informative | An end-to-end reload is requested by including no-cache in the request (or Pragma: no-cache for HTTP/1.0 compatibility). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The request includes a "no-cache" cache-control directive or, for compatibility with HTTP/1.0 clients, "Pragma: no-cache". | Provide a reload mode that sends Cache-Control: no-cache (optionally paired with Pragma: no-cache). |
| `14.9-42` | MUST NOT | A request no-cache directive must not carry field names. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Field names MUST NOT be included with the no-cache directive in a request. | Reject or never serialize no-cache="..." on requests; the request form is the bare token only. |
| `14.9-43` | MUST NOT | A server must not answer a no-cache (end-to-end reload) request from a cached copy. <br>_(server,cache [not-client])_ | The server MUST NOT use a cached copy when responding to such a request. | Server/cache-side; the client may rely on reload requests bypassing caches. |
| `14.9-44` | informative | A request max-age=0 forces every cache along the path to revalidate its own entry with the next cache or the origin server. <br>_(client,cache [CLIENT])_ | which forces each cache along the path to the origin server to revalidate its own entry, if any, with the next cache or server | Support emitting max-age=0 as the end-to-end revalidation trigger. |
| `14.9-45` | informative | Specific end-to-end revalidation is max-age=0 plus a cache-validating conditional carrying the client's current validator. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The initial request includes a cache-validating conditional with the client's current validator. | When the caller holds a validator (ETag/Last-Modified), the revalidation mode should send max-age=0 together with the conditional header. |
| `14.9-46` | informative | In unspecified end-to-end revalidation the client omits the conditional; the first cache holding an entry adds a conditional with its own validator. <br>_(client,cache [CLIENT])_ | The initial request does not include a cache-validating conditional | max-age=0 without a conditional is valid; downstream caches supply their validators. |
| `14.9-47` | MAY | An intermediate cache forced to revalidate may use either its own or the client-supplied validator without affecting semantic transparency. <br>_(proxy,cache [not-client])_ | the cache MAY use either validator in making its own request without affecting semantic transparency | Intermediate-cache-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.9-48` | SHOULD NOT | A request containing no-cache should not also contain min-fresh, max-stale, or max-age. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If a request includes the no-cache directive, it SHOULD NOT include min-fresh, max-stale, or max-age. | Emitter should refuse or warn when combining no-cache with min-fresh/max-stale/max-age on the same request. |
| `14.9-49` | SHOULD | On receiving only-if-cached, a cache should either answer from a consistent cached entry or respond 504 (Gateway Timeout). <br>_(cache [CLIENT])_ | a cache SHOULD either respond using a cached entry that is consistent with the other constraints of the request, or respond with a 504 (Gateway Timeout) status | When the client emits only-if-cached it must treat a 504 as the expected cache-miss outcome, not a transport failure to retry against origin. |
| `14.9-50` | MAY | A group of caches operated as a unified system may forward an only-if-cached request within the group. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | such a request MAY be forwarded within that group of caches | Cache-cluster-only allowance; no client obligation. |
| `14.9-51` | MUST NOT | When must-revalidate is present in a received response, the cache must not use the entry once stale without first revalidating with the origin server. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | that cache MUST NOT use the entry after it becomes stale to respond to a subsequent request without first revalidating it with the origin server | Cache-only; a client-side cache must hard-enforce revalidation of stale must-revalidate entries. |
| `14.9-52` | MUST | An HTTP/1.1 cache must obey must-revalidate in all circumstances. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | In all circumstances an HTTP/1.1 cache MUST obey the must-revalidate directive | Cache-only; no serve-stale configuration may override must-revalidate. |
| `14.9-53` | MUST | If a cache holding a stale must-revalidate entry cannot reach the origin server, it must generate a 504 (Gateway Timeout) response. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | if the cache cannot reach the origin server for any reason, it MUST generate a 504 (Gateway Timeout) response | Cache-only; client should expect 504 (not stale data) when origin is unreachable behind must-revalidate. |
| `14.9-54` | SHOULD | Servers should send must-revalidate if and only if failure to revalidate could result in incorrect operation (e.g., a silently unexecuted financial transaction). <br>_(server,origin [not-client])_ | Servers SHOULD send the must-revalidate directive if and only if failure to revalidate a request on the entity could result in incorrect operation | Origin-side emission guidance; no client obligation. |
| `14.9-55` | MUST NOT | Recipients must not take any automated action that violates the must-revalidate directive. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Recipients MUST NOT take any automated action that violates this directive | Client-side automation (retry, replay, prefetch layers) must never reuse a stale must-revalidate response without revalidation. |
| `14.9-56` | MUST NOT | Recipients must not automatically provide an unvalidated copy of the entity if revalidation fails. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | MUST NOT automatically provide an unvalidated copy of the entity if revalidation fails | On failed revalidation of a must-revalidate entity, the client must surface an error, never silently hand back the cached copy. |
| `14.9-57` | MAY | User agents under severe connectivity constraints may violate must-revalidate, but only if they explicitly warn the user that the response is unvalidated. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | user agents operating under severe connectivity constraints MAY violate this directive but, if so, MUST explicitly warn the user that an unvalidated response has been provided | If an offline/degraded mode ever serves unvalidated must-revalidate content, it must attach an explicit user-visible warning. |
| `14.9-58` | MUST | That warning must be provided on each unvalidated access. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | The warning MUST be provided on each unvalidated access | Repeat the unvalidated-response warning per access, not once per session. |
| `14.9-59` | SHOULD | The warning should require explicit user confirmation. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | SHOULD require explicit user confirmation | Prefer a confirm-style interaction before serving unvalidated must-revalidate content. |
| `14.9-60` | informative | proxy-revalidate has the same meaning as must-revalidate except it does not apply to non-shared user agent caches. <br>_(proxy,cache [not-client])_ | The proxy-revalidate directive has the same meaning as the must-revalidate directive, except that it does not apply to non-shared user agent caches. | A client-side private cache, if added, must ignore proxy-revalidate (unlike must-revalidate). |
| `14.9-61` | MUST NOT | If a message includes no-transform, an intermediate cache or proxy must not change the headers listed in 13.5.2 as subject to no-transform. <br>_(proxy,cache [not-client])_ | an intermediate cache or proxy MUST NOT change those headers that are listed in section 13.5.2 as being subject to the no-transform directive | Binds intermediaries; the client only needs to be able to emit no-transform on requests (14.9-5). |
| `14.9-62` | MUST NOT | Under no-transform the cache or proxy must not change any aspect of the entity-body specified by those headers, including the body itself. <br>_(proxy,cache [not-client])_ | the cache or proxy MUST NOT change any aspect of the entity-body that is specified by these headers, including the value of the entity-body itself | Intermediary-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.9-63` | MAY | Informational cache-extension directives may be added without changing the semantics of other directives. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Informational extensions (those which do not require a change in cache behavior) MAY be added without changing the semantics of other directives. | Client may emit extension tokens; their presence must not alter handling of the standard directives alongside them. |
| `14.9-64` | informative | The extension mechanism relies on a cache obeying all directives of its native HTTP version, obeying certain extensions, and ignoring directives it does not understand. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | obeying all of the cache-control directives defined for its native HTTP-version, obeying certain extensions, and ignoring all directives that it does not understand | Cache design principle; concrete client parse duty is captured by 14.9-65. |
| `14.9-65` | MUST | Unrecognized cache-directives must be ignored, on the assumption that standard directives alongside them keep behavior minimally correct. <br>_(cache [CLIENT])_ | Unrecognized cache-directives MUST be ignored | The Cache-Control parser must skip unknown directives without error and still apply all recognized directives in the same header. |

### 14.10-14.18 - Header Field Definitions: Connection, Content-Encoding, Content-Language, Content-Length, Content-Location, Content-MD5, Content-Range, Content-Type, Date

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `14.10-14.18-1` | MUST NOT | Connection options are hop-by-hop and must never be communicated by proxies over further connections. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | options that are desired for that particular connection and MUST NOT be communicated by proxies over further connections. | No client obligation; the client's own Connection header applies only to its immediate connection. |
| `14.10-14.18-2` | informative | Connection header grammar: a comma-list of one or more connection-tokens, each a token. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Connection = "Connection" ":" 1#(connection-token) connection-token = token | Parse and emit Connection as a 1# comma-separated list of tokens; match tokens case-insensitively. |
| `14.10-14.18-3` | MUST | Proxies must parse Connection before forwarding and remove every header field named by a connection-token. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | HTTP/1.1 proxies MUST parse the Connection header field before a message is forwarded and, for each connection-token in this field, remove any header field(s) from the message with the same name as the connection-token. | Proxy-only; no client code needed. |
| `14.10-14.18-4` | informative | A connection option is signaled solely by the presence of its connection-token in Connection, not by the presence of an auxiliary header. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Connection options are signaled by the presence of a connection-token in the Connection header field, not by any corresponding additional header field(s) | Detect options (e.g. "close") from the Connection token list itself; never infer them only from auxiliary headers like Keep-Alive. |
| `14.10-14.18-5` | MUST NOT | Headers listed in Connection must not be end-to-end headers such as Cache-Control. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Message headers listed in the Connection header MUST NOT include end-to-end headers, such as Cache-Control. | When emitting Connection, reject/never list end-to-end header names (Cache-Control etc.); only hop-by-hop options. |
| `14.10-14.18-6` | SHOULD NOT | "Connection: close" in either request or response means the connection is not persistent after the current exchange. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | in either the request or the response header fields indicates that the connection SHOULD NOT be considered `persistent' (section 8.1) after the current request/response is complete. | On sending or receiving Connection: close, mark the connection non-reusable in the pool and close it after the current response completes. |
| `14.10-14.18-7` | MUST | An HTTP/1.1 application that does not support persistent connections must send "Connection: close" in every message. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | HTTP/1.1 applications that do not support persistent connections MUST include the "close" connection option in every message. | If keep-alive/pooling is disabled, automatically add Connection: close to every request. |
| `14.10-14.18-8` | MUST | A recipient of an HTTP/1.0-or-lower message containing a Connection header must remove and ignore every header field named by its connection-tokens. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | MUST, for each connection-token in this field, remove and ignore any header field(s) from the message with the same name as the connection-token. | When a response is HTTP/1.0 and carries Connection, strip/ignore the headers its tokens name (guards against pre-1.1 proxy forwarding). |
| `14.10-14.18-9` | informative | Content-Encoding grammar: one or more content-codings as a comma-list. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Content-Encoding = "Content-Encoding" ":" 1#content-coding | Parse Content-Encoding as a 1# comma-list of coding tokens; emit the same when sending encoded request bodies. |
| `14.10-14.18-10` | informative | Content-Encoding names the codings applied to the entity-body and hence the decodings needed to recover the Content-Type media type. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | its value indicates what additional content codings have been applied to the entity-body, and thus what decoding mechanisms must be applied in order to obtain the media-type | If the client auto-decodes (e.g. gzip), apply decodings per the listed codings; otherwise expose the raw body plus header to the caller. |
| `14.10-14.18-11` | MAY | A non-transparent proxy may change the content-coding if acceptable to the recipient, unless no-transform is present. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | a non-transparent proxy MAY modify the content-coding if the new coding is known to be acceptable to the recipient, unless the "no-transform" cache-control directive is present | Proxy-only; client should tolerate a Content-Encoding differing from what the origin might have sent. |
| `14.10-14.18-12` | MUST | A response whose entity coding is not "identity" must carry Content-Encoding listing the non-identity coding(s). <br>_(server,origin [not-client])_ | If the content-coding of an entity is not "identity", then the response MUST include a Content-Encoding entity-header (section 14.11) that lists the non-identity content-coding(s) used. | Binds the response sender; client may rely on Content-Encoding being present when the body is encoded (and should mirror it on encoded request bodies). |
| `14.10-14.18-13` | SHOULD | A server should answer 415 when a request entity's content-coding is unacceptable. <br>_(origin,server [not-client])_ | If the content-coding of an entity in a request message is not acceptable to the origin server, the server SHOULD respond with a status code of 415 (Unsupported Media Type). | Server-only; client just surfaces 415 to the caller when sending encoded request bodies. |
| `14.10-14.18-14` | MUST | When multiple content codings were applied, they must be listed in application order. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If multiple encodings have been applied to an entity, the content codings MUST be listed in the order in which they were applied. | Emit multiple codings in the order applied; when decoding, undo them in reverse list order. |
| `14.10-14.18-15` | MAY | Extra encoding-parameter information may appear in other entity-header fields. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Additional information about the encoding parameters MAY be provided by other entity-header fields not defined by this specification. | No action; tolerate unknown entity-headers alongside Content-Encoding. |
| `14.10-14.18-16` | informative | Content-Language grammar: one or more language-tags as a comma-list. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Content-Language = "Content-Language" ":" 1#language-tag | Parse Content-Language as a 1# comma-list of language-tags; pass through opaquely. |
| `14.10-14.18-17` | informative | Absent Content-Language means the content is intended for all language audiences. <br>_(user-agent [not-client])_ | If no Content-Language is specified, the default is that the content is intended for all language audiences. | Interpretation default only; nothing for a protocol-level client to enforce. |
| `14.10-14.18-18` | MAY | Multiple languages may be listed for multi-audience content. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Multiple languages MAY be listed for content that is intended for multiple audiences. | Parser must accept multi-valued Content-Language lists. |
| `14.10-14.18-19` | MAY | Content-Language may apply to any media type, not only text. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Content-Language MAY be applied to any media type -- it is not limited to textual documents. | No action; do not reject Content-Language on non-text media types. |
| `14.10-14.18-20` | informative | Content-Length gives the entity-body size in decimal octets; for HEAD it gives the size a GET would have sent (no body follows). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | indicates the size of the entity-body, in decimal number of OCTETs, sent to the recipient or, in the case of the HEAD method, the size of the entity-body that would have been sent | Never wait for body bytes on a HEAD response despite a non-zero Content-Length; treat the value as informational there. |
| `14.10-14.18-21` | informative | Content-Length grammar: one or more DIGITs. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Content-Length = "Content-Length" ":" 1*DIGIT | Emit digits only; parse as a non-negative decimal integer and reject any non-DIGIT characters (framing-critical, smuggling surface). |
| `14.10-14.18-22` | SHOULD | Applications should use Content-Length to indicate the message-body transfer-length unless section 4.4 prohibits it. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Applications SHOULD use this field to indicate the transfer-length of the message-body, unless this is prohibited by the rules in section 4.4. | Set Content-Length on request bodies of known length; omit when using chunked Transfer-Encoding per section 4.4. |
| `14.10-14.18-23` | informative | Any Content-Length value >= 0 is valid. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Any Content-Length greater than or equal to zero is a valid value. | Accept 0 and arbitrarily large values (within configured resource limits); do not treat 0 as absent. |
| `14.10-14.18-24` | SHOULD | Content-Length should be sent whenever the message length is determinable before transfer, unless 4.4 prohibits it. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | In HTTP, it SHOULD be sent whenever the message's length can be determined prior to being transferred, unless this is prohibited by the rules in section 4.4. | Prefer Content-Length over chunked for request bodies of known size. |
| `14.10-14.18-25` | MAY | Content-Location may be used to supply the location of the enclosed entity when it differs from the requested URI. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | MAY be used to supply the resource location for the entity enclosed in the message when that entity is accessible from a location separate from the requested resource's URI. | Server-side option; client only needs to parse and expose the header. |
| `14.10-14.18-26` | SHOULD | A server should provide Content-Location for the particular variant returned. <br>_(server,origin [not-client])_ | the server SHOULD provide a Content-Location for the particular variant which is returned. | Server-only; client must not require its presence. |
| `14.10-14.18-27` | informative | Content-Location grammar: an absoluteURI or relativeURI. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Content-Location = "Content-Location" ":" ( absoluteURI \| relativeURI ) | Parser accepts either absolute or relative URI forms in Content-Location. |
| `14.10-14.18-28` | informative | The Content-Location value also defines the base URI for the entity. <br>_(user-agent [not-client])_ | The value of Content-Location also defines the base URI for the entity. | Base-URI resolution is user-agent/content-layer concern; protocol client exposes the raw value. |
| `14.10-14.18-29` | MAY | Future requests may use the Content-Location URI as the request-URI to identify that entity's source. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Future requests MAY specify the Content-Location URI as the request-URI if the desire is to identify the source of that particular entity. | Permission only; no code required — callers may target the Content-Location URI themselves. |
| `14.10-14.18-30` | informative | A cache cannot assume an entity with a different Content-Location can answer later requests on that Content-Location URI. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | A cache cannot assume that an entity with a Content-Location different from the URI used to retrieve it can be used to respond to later requests on that Content-Location URI. | Cache-only; irrelevant unless the client grows a cache. |
| `14.10-14.18-31` | informative | A relative Content-Location URI is interpreted relative to the Request-URI. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If the Content-Location is a relative URI, the relative URI is interpreted relative to the Request-URI. | If the library resolves Content-Location at all, resolve relative values against the Request-URI; otherwise expose raw. |
| `14.10-14.18-32` | informative | Content-Location in PUT or POST requests has undefined meaning; servers may ignore it. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | The meaning of the Content-Location header in PUT or POST requests is undefined; servers are free to ignore it in those cases. | No client action; do not attach semantics to Content-Location on outgoing PUT/POST. |
| `14.10-14.18-33` | informative | Content-MD5 grammar: base64 of a 128-bit MD5 digest per RFC 1864. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Content-MD5 = "Content-MD5" ":" md5-digest md5-digest = <base64 of 128 bit MD5 digest as per RFC 1864> | If supported, parse/emit the value as base64 of a 16-byte MD5 digest. |
| `14.10-14.18-34` | MAY | Content-MD5 may be generated by an origin server or client as an entity-body integrity check. <br>_(origin,client [CLIENT])_ | The Content-MD5 header field MAY be generated by an origin server or client to function as an integrity check of the entity-body. | Optionally support generating Content-MD5 over request entity-bodies; not required. |
| `14.10-14.18-35` | MUST NOT | Proxies and gateways must not generate Content-MD5. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | proxies and gateways MUST NOT generate it, as this would defeat its value as an end-to-end integrity check. | Proxy/gateway-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.10-14.18-36` | MAY | Any recipient of the entity-body may verify the digest against the body as received. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Any recipient of the entity-body, including gateways and proxies, MAY check that the digest value in this header field matches that of the entity-body as received. | Optional verification hook; if implemented, follow facts 37-38 and 41 for what bytes to hash. |
| `14.10-14.18-37` | informative | The MD5 digest covers the entity-body including content-coding but excluding transfer-encoding. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The MD5 digest is computed based on the content of the entity-body, including any content-coding that has been applied, but not including any transfer-encoding applied to the message-body. | Hash the still-content-coded (e.g. still-gzipped) bytes, after transfer-encoding removal. |
| `14.10-14.18-38` | MUST | A transfer-encoding on the received message must be removed before checking Content-MD5. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If the message is received with a transfer-encoding, that encoding MUST be removed prior to checking the Content-MD5 value against the received entity. | Verify MD5 only over the de-chunked entity bytes, never over the raw chunked stream. |
| `14.10-14.18-39` | MAY | Composite-type entity-bodies may contain many body-parts, each with its own MIME and HTTP headers, hashed as-is (post-encoding). <br>_(all [not-client])_ | The entity-body for composite types MAY contain many body-parts, each with its own MIME and HTTP headers (including Content-MD5, Content-Transfer-Encoding, and Content-Encoding headers). | Only matters if verifying MD5 over multipart bodies: include body-parts in the digest as transmitted. |
| `14.10-14.18-40` | informative | The Transfer-Encoding header field is not allowed within multipart body-parts. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | The Transfer-Encoding header field is not allowed within body-parts. | If parsing multipart/byteranges parts, treat a Transfer-Encoding part-header as invalid. |
| `14.10-14.18-41` | MUST NOT | Line breaks must not be converted to CRLF before computing or checking the digest; the transmitted convention stays unaltered. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Conversion of all line breaks to CRLF MUST NOT be done before computing or checking the digest: the line break convention used in the text actually transmitted MUST be left unaltered | Never normalize line endings before MD5 computation/verification; hash the exact transmitted octets. |
| `14.10-14.18-42` | informative | Content-Range grammar: header value is a content-range-spec, which is a byte-content-range-spec. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Content-Range = "Content-Range" ":" content-range-spec content-range-spec = byte-content-range-spec | Parse Content-Range on 206 responses per this production. |
| `14.10-14.18-43` | informative | byte-content-range-spec grammar: bytes-unit SP byte-range-resp-spec "/" (instance-length \| "*"). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | byte-content-range-spec = bytes-unit SP byte-range-resp-spec "/" ( instance-length \| "*" ) | Parser requires "bytes" unit, single SP, resp-spec, "/", then decimal length or "*". |
| `14.10-14.18-44` | informative | byte-range-resp-spec grammar: (first-byte-pos "-" last-byte-pos) \| "*"; instance-length is 1*DIGIT. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | byte-range-resp-spec = (first-byte-pos "-" last-byte-pos) \| "*" instance-length = 1*DIGIT | Parser accepts "first-last" or literal "*" as resp-spec; instance-length is decimal digits. |
| `14.10-14.18-45` | SHOULD | Content-Range should state the total entity-body length unless unknown or hard to determine. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | The header SHOULD indicate the total length of the full entity-body, unless this length is unknown or difficult to determine. | Server-only; client must tolerate "*" instead of a total length. |
| `14.10-14.18-46` | informative | "*" as instance-length means the total length was unknown when the response was generated. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The asterisk "*" character means that the instance-length is unknown at the time when the response was generated. | Parser must accept "bytes X-Y/*" and represent unknown total length distinctly from 0. |
| `14.10-14.18-47` | MUST | A byte-range-resp-spec must specify only one range. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | a byte-range-resp-spec MUST only specify one range | Reject Content-Range values containing more than one range (unlike Range request lists). |
| `14.10-14.18-48` | MUST | A byte-range-resp-spec must contain absolute first and last byte positions. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | MUST contain absolute byte positions for both the first and last byte of the range. | Reject suffix/open-ended forms in Content-Range; both positions must be explicit decimals. |
| `14.10-14.18-49` | MUST | A recipient must ignore an invalid byte-content-range-spec (last<first, or instance-length<=last) and any content sent with it. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The recipient of an invalid byte-content-range-spec MUST ignore it and any content transferred along with it. | Validate last-byte-pos >= first-byte-pos and instance-length > last-byte-pos; on failure, discard both header and the accompanying body content. |
| `14.10-14.18-50` | SHOULD | A 416 response should include Content-Range with a "*" resp-spec giving the current resource length. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server sending a response with status code 416 (Requested range not satisfiable) SHOULD include a Content-Range field with a byte-range-resp-spec of "*". | Server-only; client may parse "bytes */N" on 416 to learn the current length. |
| `14.10-14.18-51` | MUST NOT | A 206 response must not carry Content-Range with a "*" resp-spec. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A response with status code 206 (Partial Content) MUST NOT include a Content-Range field with a byte-range-resp-spec of "*". | Server obligation; client may optionally treat 206 with "bytes */N" as an invalid Content-Range. |
| `14.10-14.18-52` | informative | A single-range 206 carries Content-Range plus a Content-Length equal to the bytes actually transferred, not the full length. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | this content is transmitted with a Content-Range header, and a Content-Length header showing the number of bytes actually transferred. | Frame the 206 body by Content-Length (transferred bytes); never expect the full entity length there. |
| `14.10-14.18-53` | informative | Multi-range content is transmitted as a multipart message of type multipart/byteranges. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | these are transmitted as a multipart message. The multipart media type used for this purpose is "multipart/byteranges" as defined in appendix 19.2. | If the client permits multi-range Range requests, it must expect and decode multipart/byteranges responses. |
| `14.10-14.18-54` | MUST NOT | A response to a single-range request must not use multipart/byteranges. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A response to a request for a single range MUST NOT be sent using the multipart/byteranges media type. | Server obligation; client asking one range may assume a non-multipart 206. |
| `14.10-14.18-55` | MAY | A response to a multi-range request whose result is one range may be multipart/byteranges with a single part. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A response to a request for multiple ranges, whose result is a single range, MAY be sent as a multipart/byteranges media type with one part. | Multipart decoding (if any) must handle a one-part multipart/byteranges body. |
| `14.10-14.18-56` | MUST NOT | A client that cannot decode multipart/byteranges must not request multiple byte-ranges in one request. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client that cannot decode a multipart/byteranges message MUST NOT ask for multiple byte-ranges in a single request. | If the library lacks multipart/byteranges decoding, refuse (or document as caller responsibility) Range headers with multiple byte-range-specs. |
| `14.10-14.18-57` | SHOULD | Servers should return multiple requested byte-ranges in the order they appeared in the request. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | When a client requests multiple byte-ranges in one request, the server SHOULD return them in the order that they appeared in the request. | SHOULD only — a client must not hard-depend on part ordering when decoding multipart ranges. |
| `14.10-14.18-58` | SHOULD | A server ignoring a syntactically invalid byte-range-spec should treat the request as if Range were absent. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If the server ignores a byte-range-spec because it is syntactically invalid, the server SHOULD treat the request as if the invalid Range header field did not exist. | Server-only; implies client must handle a 200 full-body response to a malformed Range. |
| `14.10-14.18-59` | SHOULD | A server receiving an unsatisfiable Range (without If-Range) should return 416. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | it SHOULD return a response code of 416 (Requested range not satisfiable) (section 10.4.17). | Server-only; client surfaces 416 to the caller. |
| `14.10-14.18-60` | informative | Clients cannot rely on getting 416 rather than 200 for an unsatisfiable Range. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | clients cannot depend on servers to send a 416 (Requested range not satisfiable) response instead of a 200 (OK) response for an unsatisfiable Range request-header | Range-issuing code paths must handle a 200 full-entity response as a valid outcome of any Range request. |
| `14.10-14.18-61` | informative | Content-Type gives the entity-body media type; for HEAD, the type a GET would have returned. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | indicates the media type of the entity-body sent to the recipient or, in the case of the HEAD method, the media type that would have been sent had the request been a GET. | Expose Content-Type as-is; on HEAD, treat it as describing the hypothetical GET body. |
| `14.10-14.18-62` | informative | Content-Type grammar: a single media-type. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Content-Type = "Content-Type" ":" media-type | Parse/emit exactly one media-type (type/subtype with optional parameters, per section 3.7). |
| `14.10-14.18-63` | MUST | A Date field value is an HTTP-date and must be sent in RFC 1123 date format. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The field value is an HTTP-date, as described in section 3.3.1; it MUST be sent in RFC 1123 [8]-date format. | When the client emits Date, format it as RFC 1123 GMT (e.g. "Tue, 15 Nov 1994 08:12:31 GMT"); when parsing, still accept all three 3.3.1 formats. |
| `14.10-14.18-64` | informative | Date grammar: an HTTP-date. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Date = "Date" ":" HTTP-date | Parse Date as HTTP-date per section 3.3.1. |
| `14.10-14.18-65` | MUST | Origin servers must include Date in all responses except the three enumerated cases (1xx-optional, server-error-without-clock-convenience, clockless). <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | Origin servers MUST include a Date header field in all responses, except in these cases: | Server obligation; client must NOT hard-fail on responses lacking Date (exceptions are legal). |
| `14.10-14.18-66` | MAY | 100 and 101 responses may include Date at the server's option. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If the response status code is 100 (Continue) or 101 (Switching Protocols), the response MAY include a Date header field, at the server's option. | Client must tolerate 100/101 responses both with and without Date. |
| `14.10-14.18-67` | MUST NOT | A server without a reasonable clock must not include Date and must follow 14.18.1. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If the server does not have a clock that can provide a reasonable approximation of the current time, its responses MUST NOT include a Date header field. | Server-only; another reason the client must accept Date-less responses. |
| `14.10-14.18-68` | MUST | A recipient that will cache or gateway a Date-less message must assign it a Date. <br>_(cache,gateway [not-client])_ | A received message that does not have a Date header field MUST be assigned one by the recipient if the message will be cached by that recipient or gatewayed | Only applies if the client adds a cache; then assign receipt-time Date to Date-less responses before caching. |
| `14.10-14.18-69` | MUST NOT | An HTTP implementation without a clock must not cache responses without revalidating on every use. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | An HTTP implementation without a clock MUST NOT cache responses without revalidating them on every use. | Cache-only; irrelevant to this non-caching client. |
| `14.10-14.18-70` | SHOULD | An HTTP cache, especially shared, should synchronize its clock via a mechanism such as NTP. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | An HTTP cache, especially a shared cache, SHOULD use a mechanism, such as NTP [28], to synchronize its clock with a reliable external standard. | Cache-only; no client action. |
| `14.10-14.18-71` | SHOULD | Clients should send Date only in messages containing an entity-body (e.g. PUT/POST), and even then it is optional. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Clients SHOULD only send a Date header field in messages that include an entity-body, as in the case of the PUT and POST requests, and even then it is optional. | Do not auto-add Date to bodyless requests; at most add it (optionally) on requests carrying a body. |
| `14.10-14.18-72` | MUST NOT | A client without a clock must not send Date in a request. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client without a clock MUST NOT send a Date header field in a request. | Only emit Date when a real clock is available (practically always true; guard if clock injection allows a nil clock). |
| `14.10-14.18-73` | SHOULD NOT | A sent Date should not be later than the moment of message generation. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The HTTP-date sent in a Date header SHOULD NOT represent a date and time subsequent to the generation of the message. | If emitting Date, stamp it at (or just before) message generation; never a future time. |
| `14.10-14.18-74` | SHOULD | A sent Date should be the best available approximation of the message generation time. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | It SHOULD represent the best available approximation of the date and time of message generation, unless the implementation has no means of generating a reasonably accurate date | Use current wall-clock time at serialization when emitting Date; do not reuse stale timestamps. |
| `14.10-14.18-75` | MUST NOT | A clockless origin server must not assign Expires or Last-Modified unless those values came from a reliable clock. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | An origin server without a clock MUST NOT assign Expires or Last-Modified values to a response, unless these values were associated with the resource by a system or user with a reliable clock. | Server-only; no client action. |
| `14.10-14.18-76` | MAY | A clockless origin server may assign an Expires value known at/before configuration time to be in the past. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | It MAY assign an Expires value that is known, at or before server configuration time, to be in the past | Server-only pre-expiration technique; client must tolerate already-past Expires values. |

### 14.19-14.31 - Header Field Definitions: ETag, Expect, Expires, From, Host, If-Match, If-Modified-Since, If-None-Match, If-Range, If-Unmodified-Since, Last-Modified, Location, Max-Forwards

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `14.19-14.31-1` | informative | The ETag response-header carries the current entity tag of the requested variant with grammar ETag = "ETag" ":" entity-tag (entity-tag per section 3.11, allowing W/ weak prefix and empty quoted string). <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | ETag = "ETag" ":" entity-tag | Parse ETag values verbatim including W/"..." weak form and "" empty tag; preserve exact octets for later If-Match/If-None-Match reuse. |
| `14.19-14.31-2` | MAY | The entity tag received in ETag may be used to compare against other entities from the same resource. <br>_(client,cache [CLIENT])_ | The entity tag MAY be used for comparison with other entities from the same resource | If the client offers validation helpers, allow comparing stored ETags per section 13.3.3 rules; optional feature. |
| `14.19-14.31-3` | informative | The Expect request-header grammar is Expect = "Expect" ":" 1#expectation (a comma-separated list of one or more expectations). <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | Expect = "Expect" ":" 1#expectation | When emitting Expect, produce a 1#-list of expectation values; when the API accepts multiple expectations, join with commas. |
| `14.19-14.31-4` | informative | An expectation is either the token "100-continue" or an expectation-extension: token [ "=" ( token \| quoted-string ) *expect-params ], where expect-params = ";" token [ "=" ( token \| quoted-string ) ]. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | expectation = "100-continue" \| expectation-extension | Emit "100-continue" exactly as a token; if extensions are supported, serialize token[=token\|quoted-string] with ;-separated params. |
| `14.19-14.31-5` | MUST | A server that does not understand or cannot comply with any expectation value in a request's Expect field must respond with an appropriate error status. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server that does not understand or is unable to comply with any of the expectation values in the Expect field of a request MUST respond with appropriate error status. | A client sending Expect must be prepared to receive an error status instead of 100 (Continue); do not assume success. |
| `14.19-14.31-6` | MUST | The server must respond 417 (Expectation Failed) if any expectation cannot be met, or some other 4xx for other problems with the request. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | The server MUST respond with a 417 (Expectation Failed) status if any of the expectations cannot be met or, if there are other problems with the request, some other 4xx status. | Handle 417 on requests carrying Expect: surface the failure and do not transmit the deferred body. |
| `14.19-14.31-7` | MUST | A server receiving an Expect field containing an unsupported expectation-extension must respond 417 (Expectation Failed). <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | If a server receives a request containing an Expect field that includes an expectation-extension that it does not support, it MUST respond with a 417 (Expectation Failed) status. | Only send expectation-extensions the caller explicitly requested, and expect 417 for any extension the server does not support. |
| `14.19-14.31-8` | informative | Expectation-value comparison is case-insensitive for unquoted tokens (including 100-continue) and case-sensitive for quoted-string expectation-extensions. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | Comparison of expectation values is case-insensitive for unquoted tokens (including the 100-continue token), and is case-sensitive for quoted-string expectation-extensions. | Emit "100-continue" in canonical lowercase; preserve exact case of any quoted-string extension values. |
| `14.19-14.31-9` | MUST | The Expect mechanism is hop-by-hop: an HTTP/1.1 proxy must return 417 if it receives a request with an expectation it cannot meet. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | an HTTP/1.1 proxy MUST return a 417 (Expectation Failed) status if it receives a request with an expectation that it cannot meet. | Proxy-only obligation; client just needs to handle 417 (covered by 14.19-14.31-6). |
| `14.19-14.31-10` | MUST | The Expect request-header itself is end-to-end and must be forwarded if the request is forwarded. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | the Expect request-header itself is end-to-end; it MUST be forwarded if the request is forwarded. | Forwarding obligation binds proxies; no client action beyond not stripping Expect if the library ever relays requests. |
