| `14.19-14.31-11` | informative | Many older HTTP/1.0 and HTTP/1.1 applications do not understand the Expect header, so a client waiting for 100 (Continue) may never receive it. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Many older HTTP/1.0 and HTTP/1.1 applications do not understand the Expect header. | Implement a timeout/fallback: after sending Expect: 100-continue, transmit the body anyway if no 100 arrives within a bounded wait (see section 8.2.3). |
| `14.19-14.31-12` | informative | Expires gives the date/time after which the response is considered stale. <br>_(cache,server [not-client])_ | The Expires entity-header field gives the date/time after which the response is considered stale. | Only relevant if the client implements response caching; then use Expires to compute freshness lifetime. |
| `14.19-14.31-13` | informative | A stale cache entry may not normally be returned by a cache (proxy or user agent) unless first validated with the origin server or a fresh intermediate cache. <br>_(cache,user-agent [not-client])_ | A stale cache entry may not normally be returned by a cache (either a proxy cache or a user agent cache) unless it is first validated with the origin server | Cache-only rule; if a client-side cache is added, revalidate stale entries before serving them. |
| `14.19-14.31-14` | informative | The Expires grammar is Expires = "Expires" ":" HTTP-date. <br>_(server,cache [not-client])_ | Expires = "Expires" ":" HTTP-date | If the client interprets Expires, parse the value as HTTP-date per section 3.3.1. |
| `14.19-14.31-15` | MUST | The Expires value is an absolute HTTP-date and must be in RFC 1123 date format. <br>_(origin,server [not-client])_ | The format is an absolute date and time as defined by HTTP-date in section 3.3.1; it MUST be in RFC 1123 date format | Sender-side (origin) format rule; a client date parser should accept RFC 1123 and treat everything else per 14.19-14.31-17. |
| `14.19-14.31-16` | informative | A Cache-Control max-age directive in the response overrides the Expires field. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | if a response includes a Cache-Control field with the max- age directive (see section 14.9.3), that directive overrides the Expires field. | If caching is implemented, prefer max-age over Expires when both are present. |
| `14.19-14.31-17` | MUST | HTTP/1.1 clients and caches must treat invalid Expires date formats, especially the value "0", as already expired (in the past). <br>_(client,cache [CLIENT])_ | HTTP/1.1 clients and caches MUST treat other invalid date formats, especially including the value "0", as in the past (i.e., "already expired"). | If the client interprets Expires at all, any unparseable value (including "0") must map to "already expired", never to an error or to fresh. |
| `14.19-14.31-18` | informative | To mark a response already expired, an origin server sends an Expires date equal to the Date header value. <br>_(origin,cache [not-client])_ | To mark a response as "already expired," an origin server sends an Expires date that is equal to the Date header value. | Caching clients should treat Expires <= Date as zero freshness lifetime. |
| `14.19-14.31-19` | SHOULD NOT | HTTP/1.1 servers should not send Expires dates more than one year in the future. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | HTTP/1.1 servers SHOULD NOT send Expires dates more than one year in the future. | Server-only; no client action (clients still accept far-future dates). |
| `14.19-14.31-20` | informative | A future Expires date on a response that would otherwise be non-cacheable by default indicates the response is cacheable, unless Cache-Control says otherwise. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | The presence of an Expires header field with a date value of some time in the future on a response that otherwise would by default be non-cacheable indicates that the response is cacheable | Cache-only heuristic; apply when computing cacheability if a client cache exists. |
| `14.19-14.31-21` | SHOULD | The From request-header, if given, should contain an Internet e-mail address for the human user controlling the requesting user agent. <br>_(client,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | The From request-header field, if given, SHOULD contain an Internet e-mail address for the human user who controls the requesting user agent. | If the API exposes a From option, document/populate it with the responsible user's e-mail address only. |
| `14.19-14.31-22` | SHOULD | The From value should be a machine-usable RFC 822 (as updated by RFC 1123) "mailbox": From = "From" ":" mailbox. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The address SHOULD be machine-usable, as defined by "mailbox" in RFC 822 [9] as updated by RFC 1123 [8] | Emit From as a bare mailbox (e.g., user@example.org); do not add display names or comments that break machine parsing. |
| `14.19-14.31-23` | MAY | The From field may be used by servers for logging and identifying the source of invalid or unwanted requests. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | This header field MAY be used for logging purposes and as a means for identifying the source of invalid or unwanted requests. | Server usage note; no client behavior required. |
| `14.19-14.31-24` | SHOULD NOT | From should not be used as an insecure form of access protection. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | It SHOULD NOT be used as an insecure form of access protection. | Server-side guidance; nothing to implement in the client. |
| `14.19-14.31-25` | SHOULD | Robot agents should include the From header so the person responsible for running the robot can be contacted. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | robot agents SHOULD include this header so that the person responsible for running the robot can be contacted if problems occur on the receiving end. | For load-generator/robot use, allow (and recommend) configuring From so operators are contactable. |
| `14.19-14.31-26` | SHOULD | When a request is passed through a proxy, the original issuer's address should be used in From. <br>_(client,proxy [CLIENT])_ | when a request is passed through a proxy the original issuer's address SHOULD be used. | Keep From set to the originating user's address; proxies must not substitute their own. |
| `14.19-14.31-27` | SHOULD NOT | The client should not send the From header without the user's approval, for privacy/security reasons. <br>_(client,user-agent [CLIENT])_ | The client SHOULD NOT send the From header field without the user's approval | Never auto-populate From; only send it when explicitly configured by the caller (opt-in). |
| `14.19-14.31-28` | RECOMMENDED | It is strongly recommended that the user be able to disable, enable, and modify the From value at any time prior to a request. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | It is strongly recommended that the user be able to disable, enable, and modify the value of this field at any time prior to a request. | Expose From as a mutable per-request/per-client option, removable at any time. |
| `14.19-14.31-29` | MUST | The Host field value must represent the naming authority of the origin server or gateway given by the original URL. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The Host field value MUST represent the naming authority of the origin server or gateway given by the original URL. | Derive Host from the request URI's authority component (host[:port]), never from the resolved IP or connection endpoint. |
| `14.19-14.31-30` | informative | The Host grammar is Host = "Host" ":" host [ ":" port ] (host/port per section 3.2.2). <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | Host = "Host" ":" host [ ":" port ] ; Section 3.2.2 | Serialize Host as host with optional :port and nothing else (no userinfo, no path, no scheme). |
| `14.19-14.31-31` | informative | A host without trailing port information implies the default port for the requested service (e.g., 80 for an HTTP URL). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A "host" without any trailing port information implies the default port for the service requested | Omit ":80" for http (and ":443" for https) or include the explicit port from the URI; treat portless Host as default port when comparing authorities. |
| `14.19-14.31-32` | MUST | A client must include a Host header field in every HTTP/1.1 request message. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST include a Host header field in all HTTP/1.1 request messages | Unconditionally emit exactly one Host header on every HTTP/1.1 request; make it impossible to omit via the public API. |
| `14.19-14.31-33` | MUST | If the requested URI does not include an Internet host name for the service, the Host header must be given with an empty value. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If the requested URI does not include an Internet host name for the service being requested, then the Host header field MUST be given with an empty value. | For host-less request URIs, still send "Host:" with an empty value rather than omitting the header. |
| `14.19-14.31-34` | MUST | An HTTP/1.1 proxy must ensure any request it forwards contains an appropriate Host header identifying the service being requested. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | An HTTP/1.1 proxy MUST ensure that any request message it forwards does contain an appropriate Host header field that identifies the service being requested by the proxy. | Proxy-only; the direct-client obligation is covered by 14.19-14.31-32. |
| `14.19-14.31-35` | MUST | All Internet-based HTTP/1.1 servers must respond 400 (Bad Request) to any HTTP/1.1 request lacking a Host header field. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | All Internet-based HTTP/1.1 servers MUST respond with a 400 (Bad Request) status code to any HTTP/1.1 request message which lacks a Host header field. | Server obligation; consequence for the client is that omitting Host yields 400 — reinforces always sending Host. |
| `14.19-14.31-36` | informative | The If-Match grammar is If-Match = "If-Match" ":" ( "*" \| 1#entity-tag ); as a special case "*" matches any current entity of the resource. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | If-Match = "If-Match" ":" ( "*" \| 1#entity-tag ) | Emit If-Match as either literal * or a comma-separated list of entity-tags copied verbatim from prior ETag values. |
| `14.19-14.31-37` | MAY | If any listed entity tag matches the current entity (or "*" is given and any current entity exists), the server may perform the method as if If-Match were absent. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | the server MAY perform the requested method as if the If-Match header field did not exist. | Server processing rule; client just expects normal processing on precondition success. |
| `14.19-14.31-38` | MUST | A server must use the strong comparison function (section 13.3.3) when comparing entity tags in If-Match. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server MUST use the strong comparison function (see section 13.3.3) to compare the entity tags in If-Match. | Client implication: never place weak (W/) entity tags in If-Match — they can never match under strong comparison. |
| `14.19-14.31-39` | MUST | If none of the entity tags match (or "*" is given and no current entity exists), the server must not perform the method and must return 412 (Precondition Failed). <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | the server MUST NOT perform the requested method, and MUST return a 412 (Precondition Failed) response. | Handle 412 on requests carrying If-Match: report precondition failure without retrying blindly. |
| `14.19-14.31-40` | MUST | If the request would, without If-Match, result in anything other than a 2xx or 412 status, the If-Match header must be ignored. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If the request would, without the If-Match header field, result in anything other than a 2xx or 412 status, then the If-Match header MUST be ignored. | Server evaluation-order rule; client may still receive other error statuses despite sending If-Match. |
| `14.19-14.31-41` | MUST NOT | "If-Match: *" means the method should be performed if the selected representation exists and must not be performed if it does not exist. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | MUST NOT be performed if the representation does not exist. | Server semantics for *; client uses If-Match: * to require the resource to already exist (update-only semantics). |
| `14.19-14.31-42` | MAY | A request intended to update a resource (e.g., PUT) may include an If-Match header to prevent applying the method to a changed resource. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A request intended to update a resource (e.g., a PUT) MAY include an If-Match header field | Support attaching If-Match with a single stored entity tag on updating requests (optimistic concurrency). |
| `14.19-14.31-43` | informative | The result of a request having both If-Match and either If-None-Match or If-Modified-Since is undefined by this specification. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The result of a request having both an If-Match header field and either an If-None-Match or an If-Modified-Since header fields is undefined by this specification. | Avoid emitting If-Match together with If-None-Match or If-Modified-Since; at minimum do not generate such combinations automatically. |
| `14.19-14.31-44` | informative | The If-Modified-Since grammar is If-Modified-Since = "If-Modified-Since" ":" HTTP-date. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | If-Modified-Since = "If-Modified-Since" ":" HTTP-date | Emit If-Modified-Since as a single valid HTTP-date (RFC 1123 preferred). |
| `14.19-14.31-45` | informative | If the requested variant has not been modified since the If-Modified-Since time, the server returns a 304 (Not Modified) response without any message-body instead of the entity. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | an entity will not be returned from the server; instead, a 304 (not modified) response will be returned without any message-body. | Response parser must treat 304 as having no message-body (do not wait for or read a body after a 304). |
| `14.19-14.31-46` | informative | If the request would normally result in a non-200 status, or the passed If-Modified-Since date is invalid, the response is exactly the same as for a normal GET. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | if the passed If-Modified-Since date is invalid, the response is exactly the same as for a normal GET. | Server case (a); client consequence: an invalid date silently disables the conditional, so validate dates before sending. |
| `14.19-14.31-47` | informative | An If-Modified-Since date later than the server's current time is invalid. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | A date which is later than the server's current time is invalid. | Do not send future-dated If-Modified-Since values; they are treated as invalid and the request degrades to a plain GET. |
| `14.19-14.31-48` | SHOULD | If the variant has not been modified since a valid If-Modified-Since date, the server should return a 304 (Not Modified) response. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | If the variant has not been modified since a valid If- Modified-Since date, the server SHOULD return a 304 (Not Modified) response. | Client must correctly handle 304 for GET+If-Modified-Since (bodyless response, reuse stored entity). |
| `14.19-14.31-49` | informative | For best results, clients are advised to use the exact date string received in a previous Last-Modified header when sending If-Modified-Since (servers may use exact-match comparison). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | clients are advised to use the exact date string received in a previous Last- Modified header field whenever possible. | Store and replay the verbatim Last-Modified string as the If-Modified-Since value; do not reformat or re-round it. |
| `14.19-14.31-50` | informative | The Range request-header modifies the meaning of If-Modified-Since (see section 14.35). <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | The Range request-header field modifies the meaning of If- Modified-Since; see section 14.35 for full details. | When combining Range with If-Modified-Since, apply section 14.35 semantics rather than plain conditional-GET semantics. |
| `14.19-14.31-51` | informative | If-Modified-Since times are interpreted by the server, whose clock might not be synchronized with the client; arbitrary client-chosen dates risk clock-skew and race problems. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If-Modified-Since times are interpreted by the server, whose clock might not be synchronized with the client. | Prefer server-originated dates (Last-Modified) over client-clock-derived dates for If-Modified-Since. |
| `14.19-14.31-52` | informative | The result of a request having both If-Modified-Since and either If-Match or If-Unmodified-Since is undefined by this specification. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The result of a request having both an If-Modified-Since header field and either an If-Match or an If-Unmodified-Since header fields is undefined by this specification. | Do not automatically combine If-Modified-Since with If-Match or If-Unmodified-Since. |
| `14.19-14.31-53` | informative | The If-None-Match grammar is If-None-Match = "If-None-Match" ":" ( "*" \| 1#entity-tag ); "*" matches any current entity of the resource. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | If-None-Match = "If-None-Match" ":" ( "*" \| 1#entity-tag ) | Emit If-None-Match as * or a comma-separated entity-tag list (weak W/ tags allowed here, see -57). |
| `14.19-14.31-54` | MUST NOT | If any listed tag matches the current entity (or "*" and any entity exists), the server must not perform the method, unless required to because an If-Modified-Since date fails to match. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | the server MUST NOT perform the requested method, unless required to do so because the resource's modification date fails to match that supplied in an If-Modified-Since header field in the request. | Server precondition rule; client sees 304/412 instead of method execution when tags match. |
| `14.19-14.31-55` | SHOULD | On an If-None-Match match with GET or HEAD, the server should respond 304 (Not Modified) including cache-related header fields (particularly ETag) of one matching entity. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | if the request method was GET or HEAD, the server SHOULD respond with a 304 (Not Modified) response | On 304, read and apply the returned ETag/cache headers to update the stored validators. |
| `14.19-14.31-56` | MUST | On an If-None-Match match with any method other than GET or HEAD, the server must respond 412 (Precondition Failed). <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | For all other request methods, the server MUST respond with a status of 412 (Precondition Failed). | Handle 412 on non-GET/HEAD requests carrying If-None-Match (e.g., create-only PUT). |
| `14.19-14.31-57` | informative | The weak comparison function for entity tags can only be used with GET or HEAD requests. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | The weak comparison function can only be used with GET or HEAD requests. | Only send weak (W/) entity tags in If-None-Match on GET/HEAD; use strong tags for other methods. |
| `14.19-14.31-58` | MUST | If none of the entity tags match, the server may perform the method as if If-None-Match were absent but must ignore any If-Modified-Since fields, and must not return 304. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | the server MAY perform the requested method as if the If-None-Match header field did not exist, but MUST also ignore any If-Modified-Since header field(s) in the request. | Server rule; client consequence: when its If-None-Match tags miss, expect a full response even if If-Modified-Since would have matched. |
| `14.19-14.31-59` | MUST | If the request would, without If-None-Match, result in anything other than a 2xx or 304 status, the If-None-Match header must be ignored. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If the request would, without the If-None-Match header field, result in anything other than a 2xx or 304 status, then the If-None-Match header MUST be ignored. | Server evaluation-order rule; other error statuses can still arrive despite the conditional. |
| `14.19-14.31-60` | MUST NOT | "If-None-Match: *" means the method must not be performed if the selected representation exists, and should be performed if it does not exist (prevents PUT races). <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | the method MUST NOT be performed if the representation selected by the origin server (or by a cache, possibly using the Vary mechanism, see section 14.44) exists | Support If-None-Match: * on PUT for create-only semantics; expect 412 when the resource already exists. |
| `14.19-14.31-61` | informative | The result of a request having both If-None-Match and either If-Match or If-Unmodified-Since is undefined by this specification. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The result of a request having both an If-None-Match header field and either an If-Match or an If-Unmodified-Since header fields is undefined by this specification. | Do not automatically combine If-None-Match with If-Match or If-Unmodified-Since. |
| `14.19-14.31-62` | informative | The If-Range grammar is If-Range = "If-Range" ":" ( entity-tag \| HTTP-date ); the server distinguishes the two forms by examining no more than two characters. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | If-Range = "If-Range" ":" ( entity-tag \| HTTP-date ) | Emit If-Range as either a verbatim entity-tag or a valid HTTP-date; never anything ambiguous between the two. |
| `14.19-14.31-63` | MAY | If the client has no entity tag but has a Last-Modified date, it may use that date in an If-Range header. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If the client has no entity tag for an entity, but does have a Last- Modified date, it MAY use that date in an If-Range header. | Allow the If-Range value to be sourced from a stored Last-Modified date when no ETag is available. |
| `14.19-14.31-64` | SHOULD | The If-Range header should only be used together with a Range header. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The If-Range header SHOULD only be used together with a Range header | Only emit If-Range when the same request also carries Range; warn or reject If-Range without Range. |
| `14.19-14.31-65` | MUST | If-Range must be ignored if the request does not include a Range header or the server does not support the sub-range operation. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | MUST be ignored if the request does not include a Range header, or if the server does not support the sub-range operation. | Server rule; reinforces the client-side pairing requirement in 14.19-14.31-64. |
| `14.19-14.31-66` | SHOULD | If the entity tag in If-Range matches the current entity tag, the server should provide the specified sub-range via a 206 (Partial content) response. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | the server SHOULD provide the specified sub-range of the entity using a 206 (Partial content) response. | Handle 206 by appending/merging the sub-range with the client's existing partial copy. |
| `14.19-14.31-67` | SHOULD | If the If-Range entity tag does not match, the server should return the entire entity using a 200 (OK) response. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | If the entity tag does not match, then the server SHOULD return the entire entity using a 200 (OK) response. | A request with Range+If-Range must handle both 206 (partial) and 200 (full replacement of the cached partial copy). |
| `14.19-14.31-68` | SHOULD | If the requested resource has not been modified since the If-Unmodified-Since time, the server should perform the operation as if the header were not present. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | the server SHOULD perform the requested operation as if the If-Unmodified-Since header were not present. | Server rule; client expects normal processing when the precondition holds. |
| `14.19-14.31-69` | MUST | If the requested variant has been modified since the specified time, the server must not perform the operation and must return 412 (Precondition Failed). <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | If the requested variant has been modified since the specified time, the server MUST NOT perform the requested operation, and MUST return a 412 (Precondition Failed). | Handle 412 on requests carrying If-Unmodified-Since; treat as precondition failure, not transport error. |
| `14.19-14.31-70` | informative | The If-Unmodified-Since grammar is If-Unmodified-Since = "If-Unmodified-Since" ":" HTTP-date. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | If-Unmodified-Since = "If-Unmodified-Since" ":" HTTP-date | Emit If-Unmodified-Since as a single valid HTTP-date. |
| `14.19-14.31-71` | SHOULD | If the request would normally result in anything other than a 2xx or 412 status, the If-Unmodified-Since header should be ignored. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If the request normally (i.e., without the If-Unmodified-Since header) would result in anything other than a 2xx or 412 status, the If-Unmodified-Since header SHOULD be ignored. | Server evaluation-order rule; no client action. |
| `14.19-14.31-72` | informative | If the date specified in If-Unmodified-Since is invalid, the header is ignored. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | If the specified date is invalid, the header is ignored. | Send only valid HTTP-dates in If-Unmodified-Since; an invalid date silently disables the precondition (unsafe for updates). |
| `14.19-14.31-73` | informative | The result of a request having both If-Unmodified-Since and either If-None-Match or If-Modified-Since is undefined by this specification. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The result of a request having both an If-Unmodified-Since header field and either an If-None-Match or an If-Modified-Since header fields is undefined by this specification. | Do not automatically combine If-Unmodified-Since with If-None-Match or If-Modified-Since. |
| `14.19-14.31-74` | informative | The Last-Modified entity-header indicates when the origin server believes the variant was last modified, with grammar Last-Modified = "Last-Modified" ":" HTTP-date. <br>_(origin,client [CLIENT])_ | Last-Modified = "Last-Modified" ":" HTTP-date | Parse/retain the Last-Modified value (verbatim string) for later use in If-Modified-Since / If-Unmodified-Since / If-Range. |
| `14.19-14.31-75` | MUST NOT | An origin server must not send a Last-Modified date later than the server's time of message origination. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | An origin server MUST NOT send a Last-Modified date which is later than the server's time of message origination. | Origin-only; client may still defensively clamp future Last-Modified values when deriving validators. |
| `14.19-14.31-76` | MUST | When the resource's last modification would indicate a future time, the server must replace that date with the message origination date. <br>_(origin,server [not-client])_ | the server MUST replace that date with the message origination date. | Origin-only remedial rule; no client action. |
| `14.19-14.31-77` | SHOULD | An origin server should obtain the Last-Modified value as close as possible to the time it generates the Date value of its response. <br>_(origin [not-client])_ | An origin server SHOULD obtain the Last-Modified value of the entity as close as possible to the time that it generates the Date value of its response. | Origin-only; no client action. |
| `14.19-14.31-78` | SHOULD | HTTP/1.1 servers should send Last-Modified whenever feasible. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | HTTP/1.1 servers SHOULD send Last-Modified whenever feasible. | Server-only; client cannot rely on Last-Modified always being present. |
| `14.19-14.31-79` | informative | Location redirects the recipient to a location other than the Request-URI; for 201 (Created) responses, Location is the URI of the newly created resource. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | For 201 (Created) responses, the Location is that of the new resource which was created by the request. | Expose the Location value of 201 responses to callers as the created resource's URI (do not auto-redirect on 201). |
| `14.19-14.31-80` | SHOULD | For 3xx responses, Location should indicate the server's preferred URI for automatic redirection to the resource. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | For 3xx responses, the location SHOULD indicate the server's preferred URI for automatic redirection to the resource. | If the client implements redirect-following, use the Location header of 3xx responses as the target URI. |
| `14.19-14.31-81` | informative | The Location field value consists of a single absolute URI, with grammar Location = "Location" ":" absoluteURI. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | The field value consists of a single absolute URI. | Parse Location as a single absoluteURI per RFC 2616 (in practice, also tolerate relative references for robustness, resolved against the request URI). |
| `14.19-14.31-82` | informative | Content-Location differs from Location: it identifies the original location of the enclosed entity, and a response may contain both headers. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | the Content-Location identifies the original location of the entity enclosed in the request. | Never treat Content-Location as a redirect target; keep the two headers distinct in the response model. |
| `14.19-14.31-83` | informative | The Max-Forwards grammar is Max-Forwards = "Max-Forwards" ":" 1*DIGIT, used with TRACE and OPTIONS to limit forwarding by proxies/gateways. <br>_(client,proxy,gateway [CLIENT])_ | Max-Forwards = "Max-Forwards" ":" 1*DIGIT | When emitting Max-Forwards (TRACE/OPTIONS), serialize a non-negative decimal integer with digits only. |
| `14.19-14.31-84` | informative | The Max-Forwards value is a decimal integer indicating the remaining number of times the request message may be forwarded. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The Max-Forwards value is a decimal integer indicating the remaining number of times this request message may be forwarded. | Document/validate the Max-Forwards option as a remaining-hop count; reject negative or non-integer inputs. |
| `14.19-14.31-85` | MUST | Each proxy or gateway receiving a TRACE or OPTIONS request with Max-Forwards must check and update its value before forwarding. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | Each proxy or gateway recipient of a TRACE or OPTIONS request containing a Max-Forwards header field MUST check and update its value prior to forwarding the request. | Proxy/gateway-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.19-14.31-86` | MUST NOT | If the received Max-Forwards value is zero, the recipient must not forward the request and must respond as the final recipient. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | If the received value is zero (0), the recipient MUST NOT forward the request; instead, it MUST respond as the final recipient. | Proxy/gateway-only; client consequence: Max-Forwards: 0 elicits a response from the first hop (useful for tracing). |
| `14.19-14.31-87` | MUST | If the received Max-Forwards value is greater than zero, the forwarded message must contain an updated Max-Forwards field decremented by one. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | If the received Max-Forwards value is greater than zero, then the forwarded message MUST contain an updated Max-Forwards field with a value decremented by one (1). | Proxy/gateway-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.19-14.31-88` | MAY | The Max-Forwards header may be ignored for all methods other than TRACE and OPTIONS (and extension methods that do not explicitly reference it). <br>_(proxy,gateway,server [not-client])_ | The Max-Forwards header field MAY be ignored for all other methods defined by this specification | Recipient-side rule; client should not rely on Max-Forwards having any effect outside TRACE/OPTIONS. |

### 14.32-14.47 - RFC 2616 §14.32–14.47: Pragma, Proxy-Authenticate, Proxy-Authorization, Range, Referer, Retry-After, Server, TE, Trailer, Transfer-Encoding, Upgrade, User-Agent, Vary, Via, Warning, WWW-Authenticate

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `14.32-14.47-1` | MAY | Pragma directives are optional protocol behavior, but some systems may require behavior consistent with the directives. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | however, some systems MAY require that behavior be consistent with the directives. | No client action; treat Pragma semantics as advisory unless a specific system demands them. |
| `14.32-14.47-2` | informative | Grammar: Pragma header is one-or-more pragma-directives; directive is no-cache or an extension token with optional token/quoted-string value. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Pragma = "Pragma" ":" 1#pragma-directive pragma-directive = "no-cache" \| extension-pragma extension-pragma = token [ "=" ( token \| quoted-string ) ] | Emit Pragma per this ABNF (comma-list, token or token=value); parser must tolerate extension-pragma forms. |
| `14.32-14.47-3` | SHOULD | An application receiving a request with Pragma: no-cache should forward it toward the origin server even if it holds a cached copy. <br>_(proxy,cache [not-client])_ | an application SHOULD forward the request toward the origin server even if it has a cached copy of what is being requested. | Cache/proxy-side rule; relevant only if the client library ever grows a response cache. |
| `14.32-14.47-4` | SHOULD | Clients should send both Pragma: no-cache and Cache-Control: no-cache when the server is not known to be HTTP/1.1. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Clients SHOULD include both header fields when a no-cache request is sent to a server not known to be HTTP/1.1 compliant. | If the client exposes a no-cache request option, emit both Pragma: no-cache and Cache-Control: no-cache unless the peer is known HTTP/1.1. |
| `14.32-14.47-5` | MUST | Proxies and gateways must pass Pragma directives through regardless of their significance to that application. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | Pragma directives MUST be passed through by a proxy or gateway application, regardless of their significance to that application | Proxy/gateway-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-6` | SHOULD | A recipient should ignore any pragma directive that is not relevant to it. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | any pragma directive not relevant to a recipient SHOULD be ignored by that recipient. | Client parser must skip unknown/irrelevant pragma-directives without error. |
| `14.32-14.47-7` | SHOULD | HTTP/1.1 caches should treat Pragma: no-cache in a request as equivalent to Cache-Control: no-cache. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | HTTP/1.1 caches SHOULD treat "Pragma: no-cache" as if the client had sent "Cache-Control: no-cache". | Cache-only; relevant only if client-side caching is added. |
| `14.32-14.47-8` | informative | Pragma: no-cache as a response header is unspecified and is not a reliable replacement for Cache-Control: no-cache in responses. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | it does not provide a reliable replacement for "Cache-Control: no-cache" in a response | Client must not assign caching semantics to Pragma seen in responses. |
| `14.32-14.47-9` | MUST | A 407 response must include a Proxy-Authenticate header field carrying the challenge. <br>_(proxy [CLIENT])_ | The Proxy-Authenticate response-header field MUST be included as part of a 407 (Proxy Authentication Required) response. | On 407, client may rely on Proxy-Authenticate being present and must parse the challenge(s) to authenticate to the proxy. |
| `14.32-14.47-10` | informative | Grammar: Proxy-Authenticate is one or more challenges. <br>_(proxy [CLIENT])_ | Proxy-Authenticate = "Proxy-Authenticate" ":" 1#challenge | Client parser must accept a comma-separated list of one or more challenges in Proxy-Authenticate. |
| `14.32-14.47-11` | SHOULD NOT | Proxy-Authenticate is hop-by-hop: it applies only to the current connection and should not be passed to downstream clients. <br>_(proxy,client [CLIENT])_ | the Proxy-Authenticate header field applies only to the current connection and SHOULD NOT be passed on to downstream clients. | Treat Proxy-Authenticate as connection-scoped; never propagate it beyond the immediate hop or cache it with the response. |
| `14.32-14.47-12` | informative | Grammar: Proxy-Authorization carries credentials (single credentials value, not a list). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Proxy-Authorization = "Proxy-Authorization" ":" credentials | Emit Proxy-Authorization as a single credentials value when authenticating to a proxy. |
| `14.32-14.47-13` | informative | Proxy-Authorization applies only to the next outbound proxy that demanded authentication via Proxy-Authenticate, and is consumed by the first such proxy. <br>_(client,proxy [CLIENT])_ | the Proxy-Authorization header field applies only to the next outbound proxy that demanded authentication using the Proxy-Authenticate field. | Client must scope proxy credentials per-hop: send them only toward the challenging proxy, never toward the origin. |
| `14.32-14.47-14` | MAY | A proxy may relay client credentials to the next proxy when proxies cooperatively authenticate a request. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | A proxy MAY relay the credentials from the client request to the next proxy if that is the mechanism by which the proxies cooperatively authenticate a given request. | Proxy-only; no client action. |
| `14.32-14.47-15` | informative | Byte-range support is optional for clients and servers. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | not all clients and servers need to support byte-range operations. | Range support in the client is optional; if offered, it must follow the rules below. |
| `14.32-14.47-16` | informative | Byte ranges address the entity-body, not necessarily the message-body (i.e., before transfer-coding). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Byte range specifications in HTTP apply to the sequence of bytes in the entity-body (not necessarily the same as the message-body). | Compute and interpret range offsets against the transfer-decoded entity-body, not the chunked wire form. |
| `14.32-14.47-17` | MAY | A byte-range operation may specify a single range or a set of ranges within one entity. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A byte range operation MAY specify a single range of bytes, or a set of ranges within a single entity. | Range emitter may produce one or multiple comma-separated byte-range-specs. |
| `14.32-14.47-18` | informative | Grammar: byte-ranges-specifier is bytes-unit "=" byte-range-set; the set is 1# of byte-range-spec or suffix-byte-range-spec; byte-range-spec is first-byte-pos "-" [last-byte-pos], both 1*DIGIT. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | byte-ranges-specifier = bytes-unit "=" byte-range-set byte-range-set = 1#( byte-range-spec \| suffix-byte-range-spec ) byte-range-spec = first-byte-pos "-" [last-byte-pos] | Emit Range values exactly per this ABNF; first-byte-pos and last-byte-pos are 1*DIGIT. |
| `14.32-14.47-19` | informative | Byte positions in a range are inclusive and byte offsets start at zero. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | the byte positions specified are inclusive. Byte offsets start at zero. | Client range math: bytes=0-499 means the first 500 bytes; offsets zero-based, both ends inclusive. |
| `14.32-14.47-20` | MUST | If last-byte-pos is present it must be >= first-byte-pos, otherwise the byte-range-spec is syntactically invalid. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If the last-byte-pos value is present, it MUST be greater than or equal to the first-byte-pos in that byte-range-spec, or the byte-range-spec is syntactically invalid. | Client must never emit last < first in a byte-range-spec; validate user-supplied ranges before sending. |
| `14.32-14.47-21` | MUST | A recipient of a byte-range-set containing any syntactically invalid byte-range-spec must ignore the entire header field. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | The recipient of a byte-range-set that includes one or more syntactically invalid byte-range-spec values MUST ignore the header field that includes that byte-range-set. | Server-side rule; consequence for client: one invalid spec voids the whole Range header, so validate everything emitted. |
| `14.32-14.47-22` | informative | An absent or over-length last-byte-pos is taken as entity length minus one. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | last-byte-pos is taken to be equal to one less than the current length of the entity-body in bytes. | When interpreting 206 results, expect open-ended (bytes=N-) or oversized end positions to be clamped to entity end. |
| `14.32-14.47-23` | informative | Grammar: suffix-byte-range-spec is "-" suffix-length with suffix-length = 1*DIGIT. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | suffix-byte-range-spec = "-" suffix-length suffix-length = 1*DIGIT | Support emitting bytes=-N (last N bytes) form. |
| `14.32-14.47-24` | informative | If the entity is shorter than the requested suffix-length, the entire entity-body is used. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If the entity is shorter than the specified suffix-length, the entire entity-body is used. | Client must accept a full-entity 206 when its suffix range exceeded the entity length. |
| `14.32-14.47-25` | SHOULD | For an unsatisfiable byte-range-set the server should respond 416. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | If the byte-range-set is unsatisfiable, the server SHOULD return a response with a status of 416 (Requested range not satisfiable). | Client sending Range must handle a 416 response (range out of bounds). |
| `14.32-14.47-26` | SHOULD | For a satisfiable byte-range-set the server should respond 206 with the satisfiable ranges. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | Otherwise, the server SHOULD return a response with a status of 206 (Partial Content) containing the satisfiable ranges of the entity-body. | Client sending Range must handle 206, including partial satisfaction of a multi-range request. |
| `14.32-14.47-27` | MAY | GET requests (conditional or unconditional) may request sub-ranges of the entity via the Range header. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | HTTP retrieval requests using conditional or unconditional GET methods MAY request one or more sub-ranges of the entity, instead of the entire entity, using the Range request header | Client may attach Range to GET; combine with conditionals as needed. |
| `14.32-14.47-28` | informative | Grammar: Range header is "Range" ":" ranges-specifier. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Range = "Range" ":" ranges-specifier | Emit Range exactly as bytes=<byte-range-set>. |
| `14.32-14.47-29` | MAY | A server may ignore the Range header entirely. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server MAY ignore the Range header. | Client sending Range must also handle a plain 200 with the full entity. |
| `14.32-14.47-30` | informative | Range on a successful unconditional GET changes the status to 206 instead of 200. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | the response carries a status code of 206 (Partial Content) instead of 200 (OK). | Client must treat 206 as the success status for ranged GETs. |
| `14.32-14.47-31` | informative | Range on a conditional GET modifies the returned entity only when the condition is true; it does not affect a 304 when the condition is false. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | It does not affect the 304 (Not Modified) response returned if the conditional is false. | Client combining Range with If-None-Match/If-Modified-Since must still handle 304 with no body. |
| `14.32-14.47-32` | SHOULD | A range-supporting proxy that receives a full entity for a Range request should return only the requested range to its client. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | it SHOULD only return the requested range to its client. | Proxy-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-33` | SHOULD | Such a proxy should store the entire received response in its cache if consistent with its policies. <br>_(proxy,cache [not-client])_ | It SHOULD store the entire received response in its cache if that is consistent with its cache allocation policies. | Proxy/cache-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-34` | MUST NOT | Referer must not be sent when the Request-URI came from a source without its own URI (e.g., user keyboard input). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The Referer field MUST NOT be sent if the Request-URI was obtained from a source that does not have its own URI, such as input from the user keyboard. | If the client auto-populates Referer, suppress it for URIs not derived from another resource. |
| `14.32-14.47-35` | informative | Grammar: Referer value is an absoluteURI or relativeURI. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Referer = "Referer" ":" ( absoluteURI \| relativeURI ) | Emit Referer only as a valid absolute or relative URI. |
| `14.32-14.47-36` | SHOULD | A relative Referer value should be interpreted relative to the Request-URI. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | If the field value is a relative URI, it SHOULD be interpreted relative to the Request-URI. | If the client emits a relative Referer, ensure it resolves correctly against the Request-URI; prefer absolute form. |
| `14.32-14.47-37` | MUST NOT | The Referer URI must not include a fragment. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The URI MUST NOT include a fragment. | Strip any #fragment before emitting Referer. |
| `14.32-14.47-38` | informative | Retry-After with 503 indicates how long the service is expected to be unavailable. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | can be used with a 503 (Service Unavailable) response to indicate how long the service is expected to be unavailable to the requesting client. | Retry/backoff logic should read Retry-After on 503 and delay retries accordingly. |
| `14.32-14.47-39` | MAY | Retry-After may also appear on any 3xx response as the minimum wait before issuing the redirected request. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | This field MAY also be used with any 3xx (Redirection) response to indicate the minimum time the user-agent is asked wait before issuing the redirected request. | Redirect-following logic should honor Retry-After delay on 3xx before re-issuing. |
| `14.32-14.47-40` | informative | Retry-After value is either an HTTP-date or a decimal number of seconds relative to the response time. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The value of this field can be either an HTTP-date or an integer number of seconds (in decimal) after the time of the response. | Parser must accept both forms: HTTP-date and delta-seconds; delta is relative to response time. |
| `14.32-14.47-41` | informative | Grammar: Retry-After is HTTP-date or delta-seconds. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Retry-After = "Retry-After" ":" ( HTTP-date \| delta-seconds ) | Implement dual-form Retry-After parsing per this production. |
| `14.32-14.47-42` | informative | Grammar: Server value is one or more product tokens and/or comments. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | Server = "Server" ":" 1*( product \| comment ) | Client must tolerate Server values containing multiple products and parenthesized comments; treat as opaque. |
| `14.32-14.47-43` | MUST NOT | A proxy forwarding a response must not modify the Server header. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | If the response is being forwarded through a proxy, the proxy application MUST NOT modify the Server response-header. | Proxy-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-44` | SHOULD | Instead of modifying Server, a forwarding proxy should add a Via field. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | Instead, it SHOULD include a Via field (as described in section 14.45). | Proxy-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-45` | informative | Grammar: TE is #( t-codings ); t-codings is "trailers" or transfer-extension with optional accept-params; empty value allowed. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | TE = "TE" ":" #( t-codings ) t-codings = "trailers" \| ( transfer-extension [ accept-params ] ) | Emit TE per this ABNF; note # allows an empty field-value (TE:). |
| `14.32-14.47-46` | informative | The "trailers" keyword in TE declares the client willing to accept trailer fields in a chunked transfer-coding. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The presence of the keyword "trailers" indicates that the client is willing to accept trailer fields in a chunked transfer-coding | Send TE: trailers only if the client actually processes chunked trailers; otherwise omit. |
| `14.32-14.47-47` | MUST | Whenever TE is present in an HTTP/1.1 message, "TE" must be listed in the Connection header (hop-by-hop). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | the keyword MUST be supplied within a Connection header field (section 14.10) whenever TE is present in an HTTP/1.1 message. | When emitting TE, always also emit Connection: TE (or append TE to existing Connection list). |
| `14.32-14.47-48` | informative | The chunked transfer-coding is always acceptable regardless of TE. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The "chunked" transfer-coding is always acceptable. | Client must always be able to decode chunked responses; it cannot opt out via TE. |
| `14.32-14.47-49` | informative | Listing "trailers" asserts either all downstream clients accept trailers or the client will buffer the response for them. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | either all downstream clients are willing to accept trailer fields in the forwarded response, or that it will attempt to buffer the response on behalf of downstream recipients. | Only advertise trailers if the library either surfaces trailers to callers or fully buffers before delivery. |
| `14.32-14.47-50` | informative | HTTP/1.1 provides no way to bound the size of a chunked response for guaranteed buffering. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | HTTP/1.1 does not define any means to limit the size of a chunked response such that a client can be assured of buffering the entire response. | Do not rely on protocol-level size limits when buffering chunked responses; enforce local limits. |
| `14.32-14.47-51` | informative | A transfer-coding listed in TE is acceptable unless accompanied by qvalue 0, which means not acceptable. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | then it is acceptable unless it is accompanied by a qvalue of 0. (As defined in section 3.9, a qvalue of 0 means "not acceptable.") | Client may use ;q=0 in TE to refuse a specific extension transfer-coding. |
| `14.32-14.47-52` | informative | Among acceptable transfer-codings the highest non-zero qvalue is preferred; chunked always has qvalue 1. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | the acceptable transfer-coding with the highest non-zero qvalue is preferred. The "chunked" transfer-coding always has a qvalue of 1. | When emitting TE qvalues, expect server to pick the highest-q coding; chunked is implicitly q=1. |
| `14.32-14.47-53` | informative | With an empty or absent TE field, the only transfer-coding is chunked, and an un-transfer-coded message is always acceptable. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If the TE field-value is empty or if no TE field is present, the only transfer-coding is "chunked". A message with no transfer-coding is always acceptable. | Client not sending TE must still handle both identity (no coding) and chunked responses. |
| `14.32-14.47-54` | informative | Grammar: Trailer header is one or more field-names. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Trailer = "Trailer" ":" 1#field-name | Parse Trailer as a comma-list of header field names; emit likewise when sending chunked requests with trailers. |
| `14.32-14.47-55` | SHOULD | A chunked-encoded HTTP/1.1 message with a non-empty trailer should include a Trailer header announcing the trailer fields. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | An HTTP/1.1 message SHOULD include a Trailer header field in a message using chunked transfer-coding with a non-empty trailer. | If the client sends chunked request bodies with trailers, emit a Trailer header listing them; when receiving, use Trailer to anticipate trailer fields. |
| `14.32-14.47-56` | SHOULD NOT | If no Trailer header is present, the chunked trailer should not include any header fields. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If no Trailer header field is present, the trailer SHOULD NOT include any header fields. | Client sender: no trailer fields without announcing them; client receiver: tolerate but may flag unannounced trailer fields. |
| `14.32-14.47-57` | MUST NOT | The Trailer header must not list Transfer-Encoding, Content-Length, or Trailer as trailer fields. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Message header fields listed in the Trailer header field MUST NOT include the following header fields: | Reject/never emit Transfer-Encoding, Content-Length, or Trailer inside a Trailer list or as actual trailer fields. |
| `14.32-14.47-58` | informative | Grammar: Transfer-Encoding is one or more transfer-codings. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Transfer-Encoding = "Transfer-Encoding" ":" 1#transfer-coding | Parse Transfer-Encoding as a comma-list of codings; emit per this production. |
| `14.32-14.47-59` | informative | Transfer-coding is a property of the message, not of the entity (unlike content-coding). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | the transfer-coding is a property of the message, not of the entity. | Client must strip transfer-codings when producing the entity-body and never conflate them with Content-Encoding. |
| `14.32-14.47-60` | MUST | When multiple transfer-codings are applied, they must be listed in application order. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | the transfer-codings MUST be listed in the order in which they were applied. | Decode received transfer-codings in reverse list order; when emitting, list codings in the order applied. |
| `14.32-14.47-61` | MAY | Additional encoding-parameter information may be carried in other entity-header fields not defined by this spec. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Additional information about the encoding parameters MAY be provided by other entity-header fields not defined by this specification. | No action; unknown entity headers must simply be tolerated (general header handling). |
| `14.32-14.47-62` | informative | Many older HTTP/1.0 applications do not understand Transfer-Encoding. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Many older HTTP/1.0 applications do not understand the Transfer-Encoding header. | Do not send Transfer-Encoding (e.g., chunked request bodies) to peers known to be HTTP/1.0. |
| `14.32-14.47-63` | MUST | A server switching protocols must use the Upgrade header in the 101 response to name the protocol(s) being switched to. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | The server MUST use the Upgrade header field within a 101 (Switching Protocols) response to indicate which protocol(s) are being switched. | On 101, client must read the response Upgrade header to determine the new protocol before proceeding. |
| `14.32-14.47-64` | informative | Grammar: Upgrade is one or more product tokens. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Upgrade = "Upgrade" ":" 1#product | Emit Upgrade as a comma-list of product tokens (e.g., protocol/version). |
| `14.32-14.47-65` | informative | Upgrade cannot force a protocol change; server acceptance is optional. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | Upgrade cannot be used to insist on a protocol change; its acceptance and use by the server is optional. | Client sending Upgrade must handle a normal (non-101) response and continue in HTTP/1.1. |
| `14.32-14.47-66` | MUST | After the protocol change, the first action must be a response to the initial HTTP request that carried Upgrade. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | the first action after changing the protocol MUST be a response to the initial HTTP request containing the Upgrade header field. | After 101, client must expect the new protocol's first message to answer the original request. |
| `14.32-14.47-67` | MUST | Whenever Upgrade is present in an HTTP/1.1 message, "upgrade" must be listed in the Connection header. <br>_(client,server [CLIENT])_ | the upgrade keyword MUST be supplied within a Connection header field (section 14.10) whenever Upgrade is present in an HTTP/1.1 message. | When emitting Upgrade, always also emit Connection: upgrade. |
| `14.32-14.47-68` | informative | Upgrade applies only to switching application-layer protocols on the existing transport connection, never to a different connection. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The Upgrade header field only applies to switching application-layer protocols upon the existing transport-layer connection. | Client must perform the upgraded protocol on the same TCP connection; a cross-connection switch uses 3xx redirects instead. |
| `14.32-14.47-69` | informative | Only "HTTP" is a defined protocol name; any token may be used but is only useful if both sides agree on its meaning. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Any token can be used as a protocol name; however, it will only be useful if both the client and server associate the name with the same protocol. | Accept arbitrary tokens as Upgrade protocol names; match them case-appropriately against protocols the client supports. |
| `14.32-14.47-70` | SHOULD | User agents should include a User-Agent header with requests. <br>_(user-agent,client [CLIENT])_ | User agents SHOULD include this field with requests. | Send a User-Agent header by default on every request (configurable, but on by default). |
| `14.32-14.47-71` | informative | Grammar: User-Agent value is one or more product tokens and/or comments. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | User-Agent = "User-Agent" ":" 1*( product \| comment ) | Format User-Agent as product tokens (name/version) optionally followed by comments, most significant first. |
| `14.32-14.47-72` | informative | Vary: * means a cache cannot determine from request headers whether the response is the appropriate representation. <br>_(cache,user-agent [not-client])_ | A Vary field value of "*" implies that a cache cannot determine from the request headers of a subsequent request whether this response is the appropriate representation. | Cache-only semantics; relevant only if client-side response caching is added. |
| `14.32-14.47-73` | informative | Grammar: Vary is "*" or one or more field-names. <br>_(server,cache [not-client])_ | Vary = "Vary" ":" ( "*" \| 1#field-name ) | Parse only if caching is implemented; "*" and comma-list of field names. |
| `14.32-14.47-74` | SHOULD | A server should include Vary on any cacheable response subject to server-driven negotiation. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | An HTTP/1.1 server SHOULD include a Vary header field with any cacheable response that is subject to server-driven negotiation. | Server-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-75` | MAY | A server may include Vary on a non-cacheable negotiated response. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MAY include a Vary header field with a non-cacheable response that is subject to server-driven negotiation | Server-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-76` | MAY | A cache may assume the same selection for future requests with the same listed field values while the response is fresh. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | A cache MAY assume that the same selection will be made for future requests with the same values for the listed field names | Cache-only; relevant only if client caching is added. |
| `14.32-14.47-77` | informative | Field names listed in Vary are case-insensitive and not limited to standard request headers. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Field names are case-insensitive. | If caching is added, compare Vary field-names case-insensitively. |
| `14.32-14.47-78` | MUST NOT | Vary: * must not be generated by a proxy; only an origin server may generate it. <br>_(proxy,origin [not-client])_ | The "*" value MUST NOT be generated by a proxy server; it may only be generated by an origin server. | Proxy/origin-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-79` | MUST | Gateways and proxies must use the Via header to indicate intermediate protocols and recipients on requests and responses. <br>_(gateway,proxy [not-client])_ | The Via general-header field MUST be used by gateways and proxies to indicate the intermediate protocols and recipients between the user agent and the server on requests | Proxy/gateway-only; a pure client neither adds nor requires Via. |
| `14.32-14.47-80` | informative | Grammar: Via is 1#( received-protocol received-by [comment] ); received-protocol is [protocol-name "/"] protocol-version; received-by is host[:port] or pseudonym; names are tokens. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | Via = "Via" ":" 1#( received-protocol received-by [ comment ] ) received-protocol = [ protocol-name "/" ] protocol-version | Client may treat received Via as opaque; only tolerate its list syntax in the generic header parser. |
| `14.32-14.47-81` | informative | In Via, protocol-name is optional if and only if it would be "HTTP". <br>_(all [not-client])_ | The protocol-name is optional if and only if it would be "HTTP". | Only relevant when parsing Via entries; bare versions imply HTTP. |
| `14.32-14.47-82` | MAY | A sensitive real host in Via received-by may be replaced by a pseudonym. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | if the real host is considered to be sensitive information, it MAY be replaced by a pseudonym. | Proxy-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-83` | MAY | If the port is absent in Via received-by, it may be assumed to be the default port of the received-protocol. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | If the port is not given, it MAY be assumed to be the default port of the received-protocol. | Only relevant if the client ever interprets Via entries. |
| `14.32-14.47-84` | MUST | Each recipient must append its Via information so the result is ordered by forwarding sequence. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | Each recipient MUST append its information such that the end result is ordered according to the sequence of forwarding applications. | Proxy/gateway-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-85` | MAY | Comments in Via are optional and may be removed by any recipient before forwarding. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | all comments in the Via field are optional and MAY be removed by any recipient prior to forwarding the message. | Forwarding-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-86` | SHOULD NOT | Firewall-portal proxies/gateways should not, by default, forward names and ports of hosts inside the firewall region. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | Proxies and gateways used as a portal through a network firewall SHOULD NOT, by default, forward the names and ports of hosts within the firewall region. | Proxy/gateway-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-87` | SHOULD | Internal host information should be propagated only if explicitly enabled. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | This information SHOULD only be propagated if explicitly enabled. | Proxy/gateway-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-88` | SHOULD | If not enabled, the received-by host of any host behind the firewall should be replaced by an appropriate pseudonym. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | If not enabled, the received-by host of any host behind the firewall SHOULD be replaced by an appropriate pseudonym for that host. | Proxy/gateway-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-89` | MAY | A privacy-conscious proxy may collapse an ordered subsequence of Via entries with identical received-protocol values into one entry. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | a proxy MAY combine an ordered subsequence of Via header field entries with identical received-protocol values into a single such entry. | Proxy-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-90` | SHOULD NOT | Applications should not combine Via entries unless all are under the same organizational control and hosts already pseudonymized. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | Applications SHOULD NOT combine multiple entries unless they are all under the same organizational control and the hosts have already been replaced by pseudonyms. | Proxy/gateway-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-91` | MUST NOT | Applications must not combine Via entries that have different received-protocol values. <br>_(proxy,gateway [not-client])_ | Applications MUST NOT combine entries which have different received-protocol values. | Proxy/gateway-only; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-92` | informative | Grammar: Warning is 1#warning-value; warning-value is warn-code SP warn-agent SP warn-text [SP warn-date]. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Warning = "Warning" ":" 1#warning-value warning-value = warn-code SP warn-agent SP warn-text [SP warn-date] | Client Warning parser must split on commas then on single spaces into code/agent/text/optional date. |
| `14.32-14.47-93` | informative | Grammar: warn-code is 3DIGIT; warn-agent is host[:port] or pseudonym; warn-text is quoted-string; warn-date is a double-quoted HTTP-date. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | warn-code = 3DIGIT warn-agent = ( host [ ":" port ] ) \| pseudonym warn-text = quoted-string warn-date = <"> HTTP-date <"> | **VERIFY** Parse warn-code as exactly 3 digits, warn-text as quoted-string, warn-date as quoted HTTP-date. -- The quoted string is not contiguous in the source: between 'pseudonym' and 'warn-text = quoted-string' the RFC contains two ABNF comment lines (lines 8262-8263): '; the name or pseudonym of the server adding' and '; the Warning header, for use in debugging'. These are body text, not line-wrap/whitespace/page-break artifacts, so the quote as written does not appear verbatim. The individual productions (warn-code = 3DIGIT; warn-agent = ( host [ ":" port ] ) \| pseudonym; warn-text = quoted-string; warn-date = <"> HTTP-date <">) are each correct, but the quote must include the intervening comment lines to be verbatim. |
| `14.32-14.47-94` | MAY | A response may carry more than one Warning header. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A response MAY carry more than one Warning header. | Client must accept and preserve multiple Warning headers/values in order. |
| `14.32-14.47-95` | SHOULD | warn-text should be in a natural language and charset most likely intelligible to the receiving human user. <br>_(server,cache [not-client])_ | The warn-text SHOULD be in a natural language and character set that is most likely to be intelligible to the human user receiving the response. | Sender-side; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-96` | MAY | The language/charset decision may be based on any available knowledge (cache/user location, Accept-Language, Content-Language, etc.). <br>_(server,cache [not-client])_ | This decision MAY be based on any available knowledge, such as the location of the cache or user, the Accept-Language field in a request | Sender-side; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-97` | MUST | A warn-text charset other than ISO-8859-1 must be encoded using RFC 2047. <br>_(server,cache [CLIENT])_ | If a character set other than ISO-8859-1 is used, it MUST be encoded in the warn-text using the method described in RFC 2047 [14]. | If client surfaces warn-text, be prepared to RFC 2047-decode it; default charset is ISO-8859-1. |
| `14.32-14.47-98` | informative | Some warn-codes are cache-specific and can only be applied to response messages. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | some specific warn-codes are specific to caches and can only be applied to response messages. | Only constrains where cache warn-codes may appear; a non-caching client just parses them. |
| `14.32-14.47-99` | SHOULD | New Warning headers should be added after any existing Warning headers. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | New Warning headers SHOULD be added after any existing Warning headers. | Sender/forwarder rule; client library does not normally generate Warning. |
| `14.32-14.47-100` | MUST NOT | A cache must not delete any Warning header it received with a message. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | A cache MUST NOT delete any Warning header that it received with a message. | Cache-only; relevant only if client caching is added. |
| `14.32-14.47-101` | SHOULD | After successfully validating a cache entry, a cache should remove previously attached Warning headers except code-specific exceptions. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | it SHOULD remove any Warning headers previously attached to that entry except as specified for specific Warning codes. | Cache-only; relevant only if client caching is added. |
| `14.32-14.47-102` | MUST | After validation the cache must add any Warning headers received in the validating response. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | It MUST then add any Warning headers received in the validating response. | Cache-only; relevant only if client caching is added. |
| `14.32-14.47-103` | SHOULD | When not all warnings can be shown, the user agent should prioritize warnings appearing earlier and those in the user's preferred charset. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | If it is not possible to inform the user of all of the warnings, the user agent SHOULD follow these heuristics | Preserve response-order of Warning values when exposing them so callers can apply the priority heuristics. |
| `14.32-14.47-104` | SHOULD | Systems generating multiple Warning headers should order them with the user-agent display behavior in mind. <br>_(server,cache [not-client])_ | Systems that generate multiple Warning headers SHOULD order them with this user agent behavior in mind. | Sender-side; no client obligation. |
| `14.32-14.47-105` | MUST | Warning 110 (Response is stale) must be included whenever the returned response is stale. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | MUST be included whenever the returned response is stale. | Cache-only; relevant only if client caching is added. |
| `14.32-14.47-106` | MUST | Warning 111 (Revalidation failed) must be included when a cache returns a stale response because revalidation failed. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | MUST be included if a cache returns a stale response because an attempt to revalidate the response failed, due to an inability to reach the server. | Cache-only; relevant only if client caching is added. |
| `14.32-14.47-107` | SHOULD | Warning 112 (Disconnected operation) should be included when the cache is intentionally disconnected from the network. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | SHOULD be included if the cache is intentionally disconnected from the rest of the network for a period of time. | Cache-only; relevant only if client caching is added. |
| `14.32-14.47-108` | MUST | Warning 113 (Heuristic expiration) must be included when heuristic freshness exceeds 24 hours and the response age exceeds 24 hours. <br>_(cache [not-client])_ | MUST be included if the cache heuristically chose a freshness lifetime greater than 24 hours and the response's age is greater than 24 hours. | Cache-only; relevant only if client caching is added. |
| `14.32-14.47-109` | MUST NOT | On receiving Warning 199 (Miscellaneous warning), a system must not take any automated action beyond presenting the warning to the user. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A system receiving this warning MUST NOT take any automated action, besides presenting the warning to the user. | Client must never trigger automated behavior (retry, invalidation, etc.) from a 199 warning; surface it only. |
| `14.32-14.47-110` | MUST | Warning 214 (Transformation applied) must be added by an intermediate cache/proxy that changes content-coding, media-type, or entity-body, unless already present. <br>_(proxy,cache [not-client])_ | MUST be added by an intermediate cache or proxy if it applies any transformation changing the content-coding | Proxy/cache-only; client may use presence of 214 to detect a transformed response. |
| `14.32-14.47-111` | MUST NOT | On receiving Warning 299 (Miscellaneous persistent warning), a system must not take any automated action. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A system receiving this warning MUST NOT take any automated action. | Client must never trigger automated behavior from a 299 warning; surface it only. |
| `14.32-14.47-112` | MUST | A sender emitting Warning headers in an HTTP/1.0-or-lower message must include in each warning-value a warn-date matching the response Date. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | the sender MUST include in each warning-value a warn-date that matches the date in the response. | Only binds if the client ever sends Warning at HTTP/1.0; poseidon emits HTTP/1.1 and does not generate Warning. |
| `14.32-14.47-113` | MUST | A recipient of a warning-value whose warn-date differs from the response Date must delete that warning-value before storing, forwarding, or using it. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | that warn-date is different from the Date value in the response, then that warning-value MUST be deleted from the message before storing, forwarding, or using it. | When exposing/using Warning values, drop any whose warn-date mismatches the response Date header. |
| `14.32-14.47-114` | MUST | If all warning-values are deleted for warn-date mismatch, the Warning header itself must be deleted. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | If all of the warning-values are deleted for this reason, the Warning header MUST be deleted as well. | After filtering mismatched warning-values, remove the Warning header entirely if none remain. |
| `14.32-14.47-115` | MUST | A 401 response must include a WWW-Authenticate header with at least one challenge. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | The WWW-Authenticate response-header field MUST be included in 401 (Unauthorized) response messages. | On 401, client may rely on WWW-Authenticate being present and must parse its challenge(s) to authenticate. |
| `14.32-14.47-116` | informative | Grammar: WWW-Authenticate is one or more challenges. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | WWW-Authenticate = "WWW-Authenticate" ":" 1#challenge | Parse WWW-Authenticate as a list of one or more challenges. |
| `14.32-14.47-117` | informative | User agents must parse WWW-Authenticate carefully: it may contain multiple challenges, multiple header instances may occur, and challenge contents are themselves comma-separated parameter lists. <br>_(user-agent,client [CLIENT])_ | User agents are advised to take special care in parsing the WWW-Authenticate field value as it might contain more than one challenge | Challenge parser must disambiguate commas separating challenges from commas separating auth-params, across repeated WWW-Authenticate headers. |

### 15 - Security Considerations

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|--------------|---------------------|
| `15-1` | SHOULD | HTTP clients must take care not to unintentionally leak personal information (names, location, passwords, keys) via the HTTP protocol to other parties. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | SHOULD be very careful to prevent unintentional leakage of this information via the HTTP protocol to other sources | Audit that identity-bearing data (auth headers, cookies, custom headers) is only sent to the intended target host and never auto-forwarded cross-origin (e.g., on redirects). |
| `15-2` | RECOMMENDED | Implementations are strongly recommended to provide a convenient interface letting the user control dissemination of personal information. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | We very strongly recommend that a convenient interface be provided for the user to control dissemination of such information | Make all privacy-sensitive headers (From, Referer, User-Agent, Accept-*) caller-settable options, never silently auto-generated by the library. |
| `15-3` | informative | Parties publishing data derived from HTTP server logs are responsible for not distributing material identifying individuals without their permission. <br>_(server,origin [not-client])_ | are responsible for ensuring that such material is not distributed without the permission of any individuals that are identifiable by the published results | N/A for client code — binds server operators/content providers handling log data. |
| `15-4` | SHOULD | Applications should give the provider of sensitive information as much control as possible over how that information is transferred. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | applications SHOULD supply as much control over this information as possible to the provider of that information | Same check as 15-2: every header that can carry user-identifying data must be opt-in and fully controllable by the caller. |
| `15-5` | SHOULD | Server implementors should make the Server response header a configurable option (to avoid revealing exploitable version info). <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Implementors SHOULD make the Server header field a configurable option. | N/A — governs the server-side Server header; no client obligation. |
| `15-6` | SHOULD | Proxies acting as a firewall portal should take special precautions when transferring header information that identifies hosts behind the firewall. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | Proxies which serve as a portal through a network firewall SHOULD take special precautions regarding the transfer of header information that identifies the hosts behind the firewall | N/A — binds firewall proxies only. |
| `15-7` | SHOULD | Firewall-portal proxies should remove, or replace with sanitized versions, any Via header fields generated behind the firewall. <br>_(proxy [not-client])_ | they SHOULD remove, or replace with sanitized versions, any Via fields generated behind the firewall | N/A — Via sanitisation is a proxy duty. |
| `15-8` | SHOULD NOT | The From header should not be transmitted unless the user can disable, enable, and modify its contents. <br>_(user-agent,client [CLIENT])_ | it SHOULD NOT be transmitted without the user being able to disable, enable, and modify the contents of the field | Never emit a From header by default; only send it when explicitly set by the caller, with the ability to change or remove it. |
| `15-9` | MUST | The user must be able to set the contents of the From field via a user preference or application defaults configuration. <br>_(user-agent,client [CLIENT])_ | The user MUST be able to set the contents of this field within a user preference or application defaults configuration. | Verify From is fully caller-configurable (per-request or client-level option) and never hard-coded; absence of any From support also satisfies this. |
| `15-10` | informative | A convenient toggle interface for enabling/disabling the sending of From and Referer information is suggested but not required. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | We suggest, though do not require, that a convenient toggle interface be provided for the user to enable or disable the sending of From and Referer information. | Optional: expose a single switch that suppresses both From and Referer emission. |
| `15-11` | RECOMMENDED | It is strongly recommended that the user be able to select whether or not the Referer field is sent. <br>_(user-agent,client [CLIENT])_ | it is strongly recommended that the user be able to select whether or not the Referer field is sent | Provide a way to disable Referer emission entirely; for a library, do not auto-generate Referer unless the caller opts in. |
| `15-12` | SHOULD NOT | Clients must not include a Referer header in a non-secure HTTP request when the referring page was transferred over a secure protocol. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Clients SHOULD NOT include a Referer header field in a (non-secure) HTTP request if the referring page was transferred with a secure protocol. | If the client ever auto-sets Referer (e.g., in redirect-following logic), strip it on https-to-http downgrade; add a test for the downgrade case. |
| `15-13` | SHOULD NOT | Service authors should not use GET-based forms for submitting sensitive data, because it ends up logged in the Request-URI. <br>_(server,origin [not-client])_ | Authors of services which use the HTTP protocol SHOULD NOT use GET based forms for the submission of sensitive data | N/A — binds service/form authors, not the client implementation; clients merely transmit the caller's URI. |
| `15-14` | informative | User agents offering configuration of an always-sent Accept-Language header are strongly encouraged to warn the user of the privacy loss during configuration. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | are strongly encouraged to let the configuration process include a message which makes the user aware of the loss of privacy involved | If the client exposes a default Accept-Language option, document the privacy implication; do not send Accept-Language unless the caller sets it. |
| `15-15` | informative | A privacy-preserving approach is to omit Accept-Language by default and only offer to send it when a Vary response header shows it would improve service. <br>_(user-agent [CLIENT])_ | to omit the sending of Accept-Language headers by default, and to ask the user whether or not to start sending Accept-Language headers to a server | Default behavior check: the client must not emit Accept-Language (or other Accept-* identifiers) unless explicitly configured. |
